\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,\iota,r,b_{2},C_{T},\|\mathbf{m}\|_{\ell_{1}}}C_{g}\int_{0}^{t}(T-s)^{a_{2}}(t-s)e^{-\frac{r^{2}}{21(t-s)}}ds\lesssim_{n,\iota,r,a_{2},b_{2},C_{T},\|\mathbf{m}\|_{\ell_{1}}}C_{g}t^{3}e^{-\frac{r^{2}}{22t}}.

If
m_{n}
is odd, by (
2.16
),
{\partial}_{t}^{\iota}D_{x}^{\mathbf{m}}f_{i}((\tilde{x},0),t)=0
for
t\in(0,T]
,
i=1,2,3
. Combining
f_{1},f_{2},f_{3}\in C^{\infty}\big(\overline{B^{+}_{n}(q,r/2)}\times[0,T]\big)
, we complete the proof.
∎

2.7.
Scaling argument

Lemma 2.15
.

Let
n\geq 2
be an integer,
-\infty<t_{0}<t_{1}\leq\infty
,
v
be a weak solution of

(2.26)

\partial_{t}v=\Delta v+h_{1}(x,t)\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}\times(t_{0},t_{1}),\quad\partial_{x_{n}}v+\beta^{0}(\tilde{x},t)v=h_{2}(\tilde{x},t)\mbox{ \ on \ }\partial\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}\times(t_{0},t_{1}),\quad v(x,t_{0})=v_{0}(x)\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}.

Given
x_{*}\in\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}},t_{*}\in(t_{0},t_{1}),\rho\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, suppose
\rho\|\beta^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}\big(B_{n-1}(\tilde{x}_{*},2\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-4\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}\leq C_{\beta^{0},1}
,
v,h_{1}\in L_{\rm{loc}}^{\infty}(\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\times[t_{0},t_{1}))
,

h_{2}\in L_{\rm{loc}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[t_{0},t_{1}))
,
then
there exist positive constants
C_{1},\alpha_{0}
only depending on
n
such that if
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{0}],v_{0}\in C_{\rm{loc}}^{\alpha}(\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}})
, we have

(2.27)

\displaystyle[v]_{C^{\alpha,\alpha/2}\big(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}\langle C_{\beta^{0},1}\rangle\rho^{-\alpha}\Big[\|v\|_{L^{\infty}\big(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},2\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-4\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}+\rho^{2}\|h_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}\big(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},2\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-4\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{x_{*n}\leq 4\rho}\ \rho\|h_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}\big(B_{n-1}(\tilde{x}_{*},2\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-4\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}+\mathbf{1}_{\sqrt{t_{*}-t_{0}}\leq 4\rho}\ \left(\|v_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},2\rho))}+\rho^{\alpha}[v_{0}]_{C^{\alpha}(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},2\rho))}\right)\Big].

Under the additional assumption that for
\gamma\in(0,1)
,

(2.28)

\rho\|\beta^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}\big(B_{n-1}(\tilde{x}_{*},2\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-4\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}+\rho^{1+\gamma}[\beta^{0}]_{C^{\gamma,\gamma/2}\big(B_{n-1}(\tilde{x}_{*},2\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-4\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}\leq C_{\beta^{0},2},

h_{2}\in C_{\rm{loc}}^{\gamma,\gamma/2}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times[t_{0},t_{1}))
,

v_{0}\in C_{\rm{loc}}^{1+\gamma}(\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}})
,
then we have

(2.29)

\displaystyle\rho\|\nabla_{x}v\|_{L^{\infty}\big(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}+\rho^{1+\gamma}[\nabla_{x}v]_{C^{\gamma,\gamma/2}\big(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}

\displaystyle+\rho^{1+\gamma}\sup\limits_{x\in B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},\rho),\ \tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-\rho^{2}\},t_{*}]}\frac{|v(x,\tau_{1})-v(x,\tau_{2})|}{|\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}|^{(1+\gamma)/2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{2}\Big[\|v\|_{L^{\infty}\big(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},2\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-4\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}+\rho^{2}\|h_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}\big(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},2\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-4\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{x_{*n}\leq 4\rho}\ \Big(\rho\|h_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}\big(B_{n-1}(\tilde{x}_{*},2\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-4\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}+\rho^{1+\gamma}[h_{2}]_{C^{\gamma,\gamma/2}\big(B_{n-1}(\tilde{x}_{*},2\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-4\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}\Big)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{1}_{\sqrt{t_{*}-t_{0}}\leq 4\rho}\ \Big(\|v_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},2\rho))}+\rho^{1+\gamma}[\nabla_{x}v_{0}]_{C^{\gamma}(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},2\rho))}\Big)\Big]

with a positive constant
C_{2}
only depending on
n,\gamma,C_{\beta^{0},2}
.

Proof.

Set
\tilde{v}(z,s)=v(x_{*}+\rho z,t_{*}+\rho^{2}s)
, that is,
v(x,t)=\tilde{v}\big(\rho^{-1}(x-x_{*}),\rho^{-2}(t-t_{*})\big)
. By direct calculation,

{\partial}_{t}v=\rho^{-2}\left({\partial}_{s}\tilde{v}\right)(\rho^{-1}(x-x_{*}),\rho^{-2}(t-t_{*})),\quad D_{x}^{\mathbf{m}}v=\rho^{-\|\mathbf{m}\|_{\ell_{1}}}(D_{z}^{\mathbf{m}}\tilde{v})(\rho^{-1}(x-x_{*}),\rho^{-2}(t-t_{*}))

for
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{n},\|\mathbf{m}\|_{\ell_{1}}\leq 2
.
Then (
2.26
) is equivalent to

\left\{\begin{aligned} &{\partial}_{s}\tilde{v}=\Delta_{z}\tilde{v}+\tilde{h}_{1}(z,s)\mbox{ \ for \ }(z,s)\in Q,\quad{\partial}_{z_{n}}\tilde{v}+\tilde{\beta}^{0}(\tilde{z},s)\tilde{v}=\tilde{h}_{2}(\tilde{z},s)\mbox{ \ for \ }(z,s)\in SQ,\\
&\tilde{v}=v_{0}(x_{*}+\rho z)\mbox{ \ for \ }(z,s)\in BQ,\end{aligned}\right.

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\beta}^{0}(\tilde{z},s):=\rho\beta^{0}(\tilde{x}_{*}+\rho\tilde{z},t_{*}+\rho^{2}s),\quad\tilde{h}_{1}(z,s):=\rho^{2}h_{1}(x_{*}+\rho z,t_{*}+\rho^{2}s),\quad\tilde{h}_{2}(\tilde{z},s):=\rho h_{2}(\tilde{x}_{*}+\rho\tilde{z},t_{*}+\rho^{2}s),

\displaystyle Q:=\left\{(z,s)\ |\ z\in A_{1},\ s\in\left(\rho^{-2}(t_{0}-t_{*}),\rho^{-2}(t_{1}-t_{*})\right)\right\},\quad A_{1}:=\{z\ |\ \tilde{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1},\ z_{n}>-\rho^{-1}x_{*n}\},

\displaystyle SQ:=\left\{\big((\tilde{z},-\rho^{-1}x_{*n}),s\big)\ |\ (\tilde{z},s)\in A_{2}\right\},\quad A_{2}:=\left\{(\tilde{z},s)\ |\ \tilde{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1},\ s\in\left(\rho^{-2}(t_{0}-t_{*}),\rho^{-2}(t_{1}-t_{*})\right)\right\},

\displaystyle BQ:=\left\{\big(z,\rho^{-2}(t_{0}-t_{*})\big)\ |\ z\in A_{1}\right\}.

Obviously,

(2.30)

\displaystyle(z,s)\in Q\Leftrightarrow x_{*}+\rho z\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n},t_{*}+\rho^{2}s\in(t_{0},t_{1});\quad(\tilde{z},s)\in A_{2}\Leftrightarrow\tilde{x}_{*}+\rho\tilde{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1},t_{*}+\rho^{2}s\in(t_{0},t_{1});

\displaystyle z\in A_{1}\Leftrightarrow x_{*}+\rho z\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}.

For any
r\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, for
r>0
, denote
Q_{n}(r):=B_{n}(0,r)\times(-r^{2},0]
. For brevity,
denote
\|f_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(r)\cap SQ)}:=\|f_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}\big(\big\{(\tilde{z},s)\ \big|\ \big((\tilde{z},-\rho^{-1}x_{*n}),s\big)\in Q_{n}(r)\cap SQ\big\}\big)}
,
\|f_{2}\|_{C^{\alpha}(Q_{n}(r)\cap BQ)}:=\|f_{2}\|_{C^{\alpha}\big(\big\{z\ \big|\ \big(z,\rho^{-2}(t_{0}-t_{*})\big)\in Q_{n}(r)\cap BQ\big\}\big)}
, and

\|f_{3}\|_{C^{\gamma,\gamma/2}(Q_{n}(r)\cap SQ)}
,

\|f_{4}\|_{C^{1+\gamma}(Q_{n}(r)\cap BQ)}
are defined similarly.
Since

(2.31)

\|\tilde{\beta}^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(2)\cap SQ)}\leq\rho\|\beta^{0}(\tilde{x}_{*}+\rho\tilde{z},t_{*}+\rho^{2}s)\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n-1}(2)\cap A_{2})}=\rho\|\beta^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}\big(B_{n-1}(\tilde{x}_{*},2\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-4\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}\leq C_{\beta^{0},1},

by
[
35
, p.140 Theorem 6.44]
(see also
[
36
, Corollary 7.6]
), there exist positive constants
C_{1},\alpha_{0}
determined only by
n
such that if
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{0}],v_{0}\in C_{\rm{loc}}^{\alpha}(\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}})
, we have

[\tilde{v}]_{C^{\alpha,\alpha/2}(Q_{n}(1)\cap Q)}\leq C_{1}\langle C_{\beta^{0},1}\rangle\Big(\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(2)\cap Q)}+\|\tilde{h}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(2)\cap Q)}+\|\tilde{h}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(2)\cap SQ)}+\|v_{0}(x_{*}+\rho z)\|_{C^{\alpha}(Q_{n}(2)\cap BQ)}\Big).

We will handle the above term by term and (
2.30
) will be used repetitively.

(2.32)

\displaystyle[\tilde{v}]_{C^{\alpha,\alpha/2}(Q_{n}(1)\cap Q)}=[v(x_{*}+\rho z,t_{*}+\rho^{2}s)]_{C^{\alpha,\alpha/2}(Q_{n}(1)\cap Q)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho^{\alpha}\sup\limits_{(z^{[1]},s_{1}),(z^{[2]},s_{2})\in Q_{n}(1)\cap Q}\frac{\big|v(x_{*}+\rho z^{[1]},t_{*}+\rho^{2}s_{1})-v(x_{*}+\rho z^{[2]},t_{*}+\rho^{2}s_{2})\big|}{\big(\max\{|(x_{*}+\rho z^{[1]})-(x_{*}+\rho z^{[2]})|,|(t_{*}+\rho^{2}s_{1})-(t_{*}+\rho^{2}s_{2})|^{1/2}\}\big)^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho^{\alpha}[v]_{C^{\alpha,\alpha/2}\big(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)},

where we used (
2.30
) for the last step.

(2.33)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(2)\cap Q)}=\|v\|_{L^{\infty}\big(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},2\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-4\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)},

\displaystyle\|\tilde{h}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(2)\cap Q)}=\rho^{2}\|h_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}\big(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},2\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-4\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}.

\|\tilde{h}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(2)\cap SQ)}
is vacuum if
-\rho^{-1}x_{*n}<-4
. For
-\rho^{-1}x_{*n}\geq-4\Leftrightarrow x_{*n}\leq 4\rho
, similar to (
2.31
),

\|\tilde{h}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(2)\cap SQ)}\leq\rho\|h_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}\big(B_{n-1}(\tilde{x}_{*},2\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-4\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}.

\|v_{0}(x_{*}+\rho z)\|_{C^{\alpha}(Q_{n}(2)\cap BQ)}
is vacuum if
\rho^{-2}(t_{0}-t_{*})<-16
. For
\rho^{-2}(t_{0}-t_{*})\geq-16\Leftrightarrow\sqrt{t_{*}-t_{0}}\leq 4\rho
,

(2.34)

\displaystyle[v_{0}(x_{*}+\rho z)]_{C^{\alpha}(Q_{n}(2)\cap BQ)}\leq\rho^{\alpha}\sup\limits_{x_{*}+\rho z^{[1]},x_{*}+\rho z^{[2]}\in B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},2\rho)}\frac{|v_{0}(x_{*}+\rho z^{[1]})-v_{0}(x_{*}+\rho z^{[2]})|}{|(x_{*}+\rho z^{[1]})-(x_{*}+\rho z^{[2]})|^{\alpha}}=\rho^{\alpha}[v_{0}]_{C^{\alpha}(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},2\rho))},

\displaystyle\|v_{0}(x_{*}+\rho z)\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(2)\cap BQ)}\leq\|v_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},2\rho))}.

As a result, we get (
2.27
).

Under the additional assumption, we have

\|\tilde{\beta}^{0}\|_{C^{\gamma,\gamma/2}(Q_{n}(2)\cap SQ)}\leq C_{\beta^{0},2}
since (
2.28
) holds and

(2.35)

\displaystyle[\tilde{\beta}^{0}]_{C^{\gamma,\gamma/2}(Q_{n}(2)\cap SQ)}\leq\rho\left[\beta^{0}(\tilde{x}_{*}+\rho\tilde{z},t_{*}+\rho^{2}s)\right]_{C^{\gamma,\gamma/2}(Q_{n-1}(2)\cap A_{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho^{1+\gamma}\sup_{(\tilde{z}^{[1]},s_{1}),(\tilde{z}^{[2]},s_{2})\in Q_{n-1}(2)\cap A_{2}}\frac{\big|\beta^{0}(\tilde{x}_{*}+\rho\tilde{z}^{[1]},t_{*}+\rho^{2}s_{1})-\beta^{0}(\tilde{x}_{*}+\rho\tilde{z}^{[2]},t_{*}+\rho^{2}s_{2})\big|}{\big(\max\{|(\tilde{x}_{*}+\rho\tilde{z}^{[1]})-(\tilde{x}_{*}+\rho\tilde{z}^{[2]})|,|(t_{*}+\rho^{2}s_{1})-(t_{*}+\rho^{2}s_{2})|^{1/2}\}\big)^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho^{1+\gamma}[\beta^{0}]_{C^{\gamma,\gamma/2}\big(B_{n-1}(\tilde{x}_{*},2\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-4\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)},

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\beta}^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(2)\cap SQ)}\leq\rho\|\beta^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}\big(B_{n-1}(\tilde{x}_{*},2\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-4\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}.

Hence, by
[
35
, p.69 Theorem 4.21, and p.79 Theorem 4.30]
, we have

\displaystyle\|\tilde{v}\|_{C^{1+\gamma,(1+\gamma)/2}(Q_{n}(1)\cap Q)}\leq

\displaystyle C_{21}\Big(\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(2)\cap Q)}+\|\tilde{h}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(2)\cap Q)}

\displaystyle+\|\tilde{h}_{2}\|_{C^{\gamma,\gamma/2}(Q_{n}(2)\cap SQ)}+\|v_{0}(x_{*}+\rho z)\|_{C^{1+\gamma}(Q_{n}(2)\cap BQ)}\Big)

with a constant
C_{21}>0
only depending on
n,\gamma,C_{\beta^{0},2}
.
We will handle the above term by term.

\|\tilde{v}\|_{C^{1+\gamma,(1+\gamma)/2}(Q_{n}(1)\cap Q)}=\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(1)\cap Q)}+\|\nabla_{z}\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(1)\cap Q)}+[\nabla_{z}\tilde{v}]_{C^{\gamma,\gamma/2}(Q_{n}(1)\cap Q)}+[\tilde{v}]_{C_{t}^{(1+\gamma)/2}(Q_{n}(1)\cap Q)}.

Therein, by (
2.30
),

\|\nabla_{z}\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(1)\cap Q)}=\rho\|(\nabla_{x}v)(x_{*}+\rho z,t_{*}+\rho^{2}s)\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(1)\cap Q)}=\rho\|\nabla_{x}v\|_{L^{\infty}\big(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}.

Similar to (
2.32
),

[\nabla_{z}\tilde{v}]_{C^{\gamma,\gamma/2}(Q_{n}(1)\cap Q)}=\rho[(\nabla_{x}v)(x_{*}+\rho z,t_{*}+\rho^{2}s)]_{C^{\gamma,\gamma/2}(Q_{n}(1)\cap Q)}=\rho^{1+\gamma}[\nabla_{x}v]_{C^{\gamma,\gamma/2}\big(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}.

\displaystyle[\tilde{v}]_{C_{t}^{(1+\gamma)/2}(Q_{n}(1)\cap Q)}=\rho^{1+\gamma}\sup\limits_{(z,s_{1}),(z,s_{2})\in Q_{n}(1)\cap Q}\frac{|v(x_{*}+\rho z,t_{*}+\rho^{2}s_{1})-v(x_{*}+\rho z,t_{*}+\rho^{2}s_{2})|}{|(t_{*}+\rho^{2}s_{1})-(t_{*}+\rho^{2}s_{2})|^{(1+\gamma)/2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho^{1+\gamma}\sup\limits_{x\in B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},\rho),\ \tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-\rho^{2}\},t_{*}]}\frac{|v(x,\tau_{1})-v(x,\tau_{2})|}{|\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}|^{(1+\gamma)/2}}.

\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(2)\cap Q)},\|\tilde{h}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(2)\cap Q)}
have been handled in (
2.33
).

\|\tilde{h}_{2}\|_{C^{\gamma,\gamma/2}(Q_{n}(2)\cap SQ)}
is vacuum if
-\rho^{-1}x_{*n}<-4
. For
-\rho^{-1}x_{*n}\geq-4\Leftrightarrow x_{*n}\leq 4\rho
, similar to (
2.35
),

\displaystyle[\tilde{h}_{2}]_{C^{\gamma,\gamma/2}(Q_{n}(2)\cap SQ)}\leq\rho^{1+\gamma}[h_{2}]_{C^{\gamma,\gamma/2}\big(B_{n-1}(\tilde{x}_{*},2\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-4\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)},

\displaystyle\|\tilde{h}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(2)\cap SQ)}\leq\rho\|h_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}\big(B_{n-1}(\tilde{x}_{*},2\rho)\times\big(\max\{t_{0},t_{*}-4\rho^{2}\},t_{*}\big]\big)}.

\|v_{0}(x_{*}+\rho z)\|_{C^{1+\gamma}(Q_{n}(2)\cap BQ)}
is vacuum if
\rho^{-2}(t_{0}-t_{*})<-16
.
For
\rho^{-2}(t_{0}-t_{*})\geq-16\Leftrightarrow\sqrt{t_{*}-t_{0}}\leq 4\rho
, similar to (
2.34
), we have

\displaystyle\|v_{0}(x_{*}+\rho z)\|_{C^{1+\gamma}(Q_{n}(2)\cap BQ)}\sim_{\gamma}\|v_{0}(x_{*}+\rho z)\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{n}(2)\cap BQ)}+\rho[(\nabla_{x}v_{0})(x_{*}+\rho z)]_{C^{\gamma}(Q_{n}(2)\cap BQ)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|v_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},2\rho))}+\rho^{1+\gamma}[\nabla_{x}v_{0}]_{C^{\gamma}(B_{n}^{+}(x_{*},2\rho))}.

In sum, we obtain (
2.29
). ∎

3.
Approximate solution and inner-outer gluing scheme

The finite-time blow-up solutions of heat equations with the critical boundary condition are expected to behave like the dilation of steady-state solutions. Given an integer
\mathfrak{o}\geq 1
, we define

U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x):=\mu_{i}^{-\frac{n-2}{2}}U\left(\frac{\tilde{x}-\xi^{[i]}}{\mu_{i}},\frac{x_{n}}{\mu_{i}}\right),\quad\tilde{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1},\ x_{n}\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\ x=\left(\tilde{x},x_{n}\right),\quad i=1,2,\dots,\mathfrak{o}

with
\mu_{i}=\mu_{i}(t)\in C^{1}\left([0,T),\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)
,
\xi^{[i]}=\xi^{[i]}(t)=\big(\xi_{1}^{[i]}(t),\xi_{2}^{[i]}(t),\dots,\xi_{n-1}^{[i]}(t)\big)\in C^{1}\left([0,T),\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\right)
to be determined later.
Given arbitrary
\mathfrak{o}
distinct points
q^{[i]}
on
\partial\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}
,
i=1,2,\dots,\mathfrak{o}
, we define

x^{[i]}:=x-q^{[i]},\quad y^{[i]}:=\frac{x-(\xi^{[i]},0)}{\mu_{i}},\quad z^{[i]}:=\frac{x-q^{[i]}}{\sqrt{T-t}},\quad\delta:=\frac{1}{32}\min\limits_{1\leq i\neq j\leq\mathfrak{o}}|q^{[i]}-q^{[j]}|,

and some cut-off functions

(3.1)

\eta_{R}(y^{[i]}):=\eta(\frac{y^{[i]}}{R})\mbox{ \ with \ }R=|\ln{T}|,\quad\eta_{C\delta}(x^{[i]}):=\eta(\frac{x^{[i]}}{C\delta})\mbox{ \ for \ }C>0.

Given
\mathfrak{o}
integers
l_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
(could be duplicated),
i=1,2,\dots,\mathfrak{o}
, denote
l_{\rm{max}}:=\max\limits_{i=1,2,\dots,\mathfrak{o}}l_{i}
.
We look for the solution of the form

u=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{o}}\left(U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\eta_{2\delta}(x^{[i]})+\Theta_{l_{i}}(x^{[i]},t)\eta_{\delta}(x^{[i]})+\mu_{i}^{-\frac{n-2}{2}}\phi_{i}(y^{[i]},t)\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})\right)+\psi(x,t),

where
\Theta_{l_{i}}
is given in (
1.14
) satisfying (
1.15
) and
\phi_{i},\psi
will be determined later.
Denote

{\bm{\mu}}=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\dots,\mu_{\mathfrak{o}}),\quad\bm{\xi}=(\xi^{[1]},\xi^{[2]},\dots,\xi^{[\mathfrak{o}]}),\quad\bm{\phi}=(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\dots,\phi_{\mathfrak{o}}).

We introduce the error operators as

\mathcal{E}_{1}[u]:=-{\partial}_{t}u+\Delta_{x}u\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(0,T),\qquad\mathcal{E}_{2}[u]:={\partial}_{x_{n}}u+|u|^{\frac{2}{n-2}}u\mbox{ \ on \ }\partial\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(0,T).

Solving (
1.1
) is equivalent to making
\mathcal{E}_{1}[u]=0
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}[u]=0
. By (
1.15
),
direct calculations give that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}[u]=\left(-\partial_{t}+\Delta_{x}\right)\psi+\sum_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{o}}\Big[\mathcal{E}_{1}\left[U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right]\eta_{2\delta}(x^{[i]})+\mathcal{E}_{U,i}^{\rm{cut}}+\mathcal{E}_{\Theta,i}^{\rm{cut}}

\displaystyle+\mu_{i}^{-\frac{n+2}{2}}\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})\left(-\mu_{i}^{2}{\partial}_{t}\phi_{i}(y^{[i]},t)+\Delta_{y^{[i]}}\phi_{i}(y^{[i]},t)\right)+\Lambda_{1,i}[\phi_{i},\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}]+\Lambda_{2,i}[\phi_{i},\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}]\Big],

where

(3.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}\left[U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right]=\dot{\mu}_{i}\mu_{i}^{-\frac{n}{2}}Z_{n}(y^{[i]})+\mu_{i}^{-\frac{n}{2}}\dot{\xi}^{[i]}\cdot\left(\nabla_{\tilde{y}^{[i]}}U\right)(\tilde{y}^{[i]},y_{n}^{[i]}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{U,i}^{\rm{cut}}:=2\nabla_{x}\left(U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right)\cdot\nabla_{x}\left(\eta_{2\delta}(x^{[i]})\right)+U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\Delta_{x}\left(\eta_{2\delta}(x^{[i]})\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\Theta,i}^{\rm{cut}}:=2\nabla_{x}\left(\Theta_{l_{i}}(x^{[i]},t)\right)\cdot\nabla_{x}\left(\eta_{\delta}(x^{[i]})\right)+\Theta_{l_{i}}(x^{[i]},t)\Delta_{x}\left(\eta_{\delta}(x^{[i]})\right),

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1,i}[\phi_{i},\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}]:=\mu_{i}^{-\frac{n-2}{2}}\left[\frac{\dot{\xi}^{[i]}}{\mu_{i}R}\cdot\left(\nabla_{\tilde{x}}\eta\right)(\frac{\tilde{y}^{[i]}}{R},\frac{y_{n}^{[i]}}{R})+\frac{(\mu_{i}R)^{\prime}}{\mu_{i}R}\frac{y^{[i]}}{R}\cdot\left(\nabla_{x}\eta\right)(\frac{y^{[i]}}{R})\right]\phi_{i}(y^{[i]},t)

\displaystyle+\mu_{i}^{-\frac{n+2}{2}}R^{-2}\phi_{i}(y^{[i]},t)\left(\Delta_{x}\eta\right)(\frac{y^{[i]}}{R})+2\mu_{i}^{-\frac{n+2}{2}}R^{-1}\nabla_{y^{[i]}}\phi_{i}(y^{[i]},t)\cdot\left(\nabla_{x}\eta\right)(\frac{y^{[i]}}{R}),

\displaystyle\Lambda_{2,i}[\phi_{i},\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}]:=\mu_{i}^{-\frac{n-2}{2}}\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})\left[\mu_{i}^{-1}\dot{\xi}^{[i]}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{y}^{[i]}}\phi_{i}(y^{[i]},t)+\dot{\mu}_{i}\mu_{i}^{-1}\left(\frac{n-2}{2}\phi_{i}(y^{[i]},t)+y^{[i]}\cdot\nabla_{y^{[i]}}\phi_{i}(y^{[i]},t)\right)\right].

Since
\eta(x)
is a smooth radial cut-off function, we have
\partial_{x_{n}}f\big|_{x_{n}=0}=0
on
\partial\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(0,T)
for
f=\eta_{R}(y^{[i]}),\eta_{\delta}(x^{[i]}),\eta_{2\delta}(x^{[i]})
. Combining (
1.11
) for
U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}
and (
1.15
) for
\Theta_{l_{i}}
, we have

(3.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}[u]=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{o}}\Big[-\left(U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right)^{\frac{n}{n-2}}\eta_{2\delta}(x^{[i]})+\mu_{i}^{-\frac{n}{2}}\eta_{R}(y^{[i]}){\partial}_{y_{n}^{[i]}}\phi_{i}(y^{[i]},t)\Big]+\partial_{x_{n}}\psi+|u|^{\frac{2}{n-2}}u

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{o}}\Big[\left(U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right)^{\frac{n}{n-2}}\left(\eta_{2\delta}^{\frac{n}{n-2}}(x^{[i]})-\eta_{2\delta}(x^{[i]})\right)+\mu_{i}^{-\frac{n}{2}}\eta_{R}(y^{[i]}){\partial}_{y_{n}^{[i]}}\phi_{i}(y^{[i]},t)\Big]+\partial_{x_{n}}\psi

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{o}}\frac{n}{n-2}\left(U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right)^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\eta_{2\delta}^{\frac{2}{n-2}}(x^{[i]})\left(\Theta_{l_{i}}(x^{[i]},t)\eta_{\delta}(x^{[i]})+\mu_{i}^{-\frac{n-2}{2}}\phi_{i}(y^{[i]},t)\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})+\psi(x,t)\right)+\mathcal{N}\left[\psi,\bm{\phi},\bm{\mu},\bm{\xi}\right]

with the nonlinear term

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}\left[\psi,\bm{\phi},\bm{\mu},\bm{\xi}\right]:=|u|^{\frac{2}{n-2}}u-\sum_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{o}}\left(U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right)^{\frac{n}{n-2}}\eta_{2\delta}^{\frac{n}{n-2}}(x^{[i]})

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{o}}\frac{n}{n-2}\left(U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right)^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\eta_{2\delta}^{\frac{2}{n-2}}(x^{[i]})\left(\Theta_{l_{i}}(x^{[i]},t)\eta_{\delta}(x^{[i]})+\mu_{i}^{-\frac{n-2}{2}}\phi_{i}(y^{[i]},t)\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})+\psi(x,t)\right).

In order to make
\mathcal{E}_{1}[u]=0
in
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}\times\left(0,T\right)
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}[u]=0
on
\partial\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}\times\left(0,T\right)
, it suffices to solve the following inner-outer gluing system.

The inner problems:
For
i=1,2,\dots,\mathfrak{o}
,

(3.4)

\begin{cases}\mu_{i}^{2}{\partial}_{t}\phi_{i}=\Delta_{y^{[i]}}\phi_{i}+\mathcal{H}_{1,i}[\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}]&\mbox{ \ for \ }t\in\left(0,T\right),y^{[i]}\in B_{n}^{+}(0,2R),\\
-{\partial}_{y_{n}^{[i]}}\phi_{i}=\frac{n}{n-2}U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}(y^{[i]})\phi_{i}+\mathcal{H}_{2,i}[\psi,\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}]&\mbox{ \ for \ }t\in\left(0,T\right),y^{[i]}\in B_{n-1}(0,2R)\times\{0\},\end{cases}

where

(3.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{1,i}[\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}]:=\eta\Big(\frac{y^{[i]}}{4R}\Big)\mu_{i}^{\frac{n+2}{2}}\mathcal{E}_{1}\left[U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right]=\eta\Big(\frac{y^{[i]}}{4R}\Big)\Big(\dot{\mu}_{i}\mu_{i}Z_{n}(y^{[i]})+\mu_{i}\dot{\xi}^{[i]}\cdot\left(\nabla_{\tilde{y}^{[i]}}U\right)(\tilde{y}^{[i]},y_{n}^{[i]})\Big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{2,i}[\psi,\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}]:=\frac{n}{n-2}\mu_{i}^{\frac{n}{2}-1}U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}(\tilde{y}^{[i]},0)\eta\Big(\frac{\tilde{y}^{[i]}}{4R},0\Big)\Big(\Theta_{l_{i}}\big((\mu_{i}\tilde{y}^{[i]}+\xi^{[i]}-\tilde{q}^{[i]},0),t\big)+\psi\big((\mu_{i}\tilde{y}^{[i]}+\xi^{[i]},0),t\big)\Big).

The outer problem:

(3.6)

\partial_{t}\psi=\Delta_{x}\psi+\mathcal{G}_{1}[\bm{\phi},\bm{\mu},\bm{\xi}]\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}\times\left(0,T\right),\quad-\partial_{x_{n}}\psi=\mathcal{G}_{2}[\psi,\bm{\phi},\bm{\mu},\bm{\xi}]\mbox{ \ on \ }\partial\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}\times\left(0,T\right),

where

(3.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1}[\bm{\phi},\bm{\mu},\bm{\xi}]:=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{o}}\Big[\mathcal{E}_{1}\left[U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right]\left(\eta_{2\delta}(x^{[i]})-\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})\right)+\mathcal{E}_{U,i}^{\rm{cut}}+\mathcal{E}_{\Theta,i}^{\rm{cut}}+\Lambda_{1,i}[\phi_{i},\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}]+\Lambda_{2,i}[\phi_{i},\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}]\Big],

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{2}[\psi,\bm{\phi},\bm{\mu},\bm{\xi}]:=\bigg[\mathcal{N}\left[\psi,\bm{\phi},\bm{\mu},\bm{\xi}\right]+\sum_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{o}}\Big\{\left(U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right)^{\frac{n}{n-2}}\left(\eta_{2\delta}^{\frac{n}{n-2}}(x^{[i]})-\eta_{2\delta}(x^{[i]})\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{n}{n-2}\left(U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right)^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\Big[\left(\eta_{\delta}(x^{[i]})-\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})\right)\Theta_{l_{i}}(x^{[i]},t)+\left(\eta_{2\delta}^{\frac{2}{n-2}}(x^{[i]})-\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})\right)\psi(x,t)

\displaystyle+\left(\eta_{2\delta}^{\frac{2}{n-2}}(x^{[i]})-1\right)\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})\mu_{i}^{-\frac{n-2}{2}}\phi_{i}(y^{[i]},t)\Big]\Big\}\bigg]\bigg|_{x_{n}=0}.

In order to avoid the difficulty of the compactness argument due to the singularity of right-hand sides as
t\uparrow T
, we will solve (
3.6
), (
3.4
) for
t\in(0,T_{\sigma_{0}})
with
T_{\sigma_{0}}:=T-\sigma_{0},\sigma_{0}\in(0,T)
instead of
\left(0,T\right)
. Details will be given in Section
7
. For applying Proposition
1.2
in
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}
for solving
(
3.4
), we impose the cut-off functions
\eta(\frac{y^{[i]}}{4R}),\eta(\frac{\tilde{y}^{[i]}}{4R},0)
to restrict the spatial growth of
\mathcal{H}_{1,i},\mathcal{H}_{2,i}
respectively.

4.
Proof of Proposition
1.2

In this section, we will prove Proposition
1.2
, which will be used for solving the inner problems.
The following nondegeneracy lemma is prepared for the forthcoming blow-up argument.

Lemma 4.1
.

For an integer
n\geq 3
, all bounded solutions of the equation
\Delta\phi=0
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
,
-{\partial}_{x_{n}}\phi=\frac{n}{n-2}U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\phi
on
\partial\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
are the linear combination of
Z_{j}(x)
,
j=1,2,\dots,n
, given in (
2.1
).

Proof.

Since
(-\Delta_{\tilde{x}})^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi(\tilde{x},0)-\frac{n}{n-2}\left(U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\phi\right)(\tilde{x},0)=0
in
\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
and
[
10
, Theorem 1.1]
, we have
\phi(\tilde{x},0)=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}c_{j}Z_{j}(\tilde{x},0)
for some
c_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
, and thus
\phi(x)=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}c_{j}Z_{j}(x)
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
, where we used the fact that the bounded solution of the equation
\Delta u=0
in
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}
,

u=0
on
{\partial}\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}
is zero.
∎

Recall the norms defined in (
1.17
).

Proposition 4.2
.

Given an integer
n\geq 5
, consider

(4.1)

{\partial}_{\tau}\phi=\Delta\phi+g\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}),\quad-{\partial}_{y_{n}}\phi=\frac{n}{n-2}U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\phi+h\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}),\quad\phi(y,\tau_{0})=0\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+},

where
\phi
is given by

(4.2)

\phi(y,\tau)=\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}G_{n}(y,\tau,z,s)g(z,s)dzds+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}G_{n}(y,\tau,(\tilde{z},0),s)\left[\frac{n}{n-2}\left(U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\phi\right)((\tilde{z},0),s)+h(\tilde{z},s)\right]d\tilde{z}ds

with
G_{n}
given in (
2.15
).
Suppose that
1\leq\tau_{0}<\tau_{1}\leq\infty
,
\ell(\tau)
satisfies
C_{\ell}^{-1}\tau^{p}\leq\ell(\tau)\leq C_{\ell}\tau^{p}
with a constant
C_{\ell}\geq 1
,

(4.3)

2<a<n-2,\quad a^{-1}<p\leq\frac{1}{2},\quad\iota\in(0,\frac{1}{4}),\quad\sigma-pa+2\iota n>0,\quad\varsigma\in(0,1),

\|g\|_{\sigma,2+a,\ell(\tau),\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}<\infty
,
\|h\|_{\sigma,1+a,\ell(\tau),\varsigma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}<\infty
, and

g=g(y,\tau)
,
h=h(\tilde{y},\tau)
satisfy the orthogonality conditions

(4.4)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}g(y,\tau)Z_{j}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}h(\tilde{y},\tau)Z_{j}(\tilde{y},0)d\tilde{y}=0\quad\mbox{ \ for \ }\tau\in(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}),\quad j=0,1,\dots,n

with
Z_{j}
given in (
2.1
) and (
2.3
), then there exists a unique solution
\phi
in
L^{\infty}\big(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(\tau_{0},\tilde{\tau})\big)
for all
\tilde{\tau}\in(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\cap(\tau_{0},\infty)
and satisfying

(4.5)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\phi(y,\tau)Z_{j}(y)dy=0\quad\mbox{ \ for \ }\tau\in(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}),\quad j=0,1,\dots,n,

and

(4.6)

\displaystyle|\phi|\lesssim

\displaystyle\Big(\tau^{\sigma}\langle y\rangle^{-a}\mathbf{1}_{|y|\leq\ell(\tau)}+\tau^{\sigma}\ell^{-a}(\tau)e^{-\iota\frac{|y|^{2}}{\tau}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|>\ell(\tau)}\Big)\Big(\|g\|_{\sigma,2+a,\ell(\tau),\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}+\|h\|_{\sigma,1+a,\ell(\tau),\mathbb{R}^{n-1},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}\Big),

\displaystyle|\nabla\phi|\lesssim

\displaystyle\Big(\tau^{\sigma}\langle y\rangle^{-1-a}\mathbf{1}_{|y|\leq\ell(\tau)}+\tau^{\sigma}\ell^{-a}(\tau)|y|^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{\ell(\tau)<|y|\leq\tau^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\tau^{\sigma-\frac{1}{2}}\ell^{-a}(\tau)e^{-\tilde{\iota}\frac{|y|^{2}}{\tau}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|>\tau^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big(\|g\|_{\sigma,2+a,\ell(\tau),\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}+\|h\|_{\sigma,1+a,\ell(\tau),\varsigma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}\Big)

with a constant
\tilde{\iota}\in(0,\iota)
, where both “
\lesssim
” are independent of
\tau_{0},\tau_{1},g,h
.

We first use Proposition
4.2
to deduce Proposition
1.2
.

Proof of Proposition
1.2
.

Set
\phi(y,\tau)=\phi_{1}(y,\tau)+c(\tau)\tilde{Z}_{0}(y)
. It suffices to consider

{\partial}_{\tau}\phi_{1}=\Delta\phi_{1}+g_{1}\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}),\quad-{\partial}_{y_{n}}\phi_{1}=\frac{n}{n-2}U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\phi_{1}+h_{1}\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}),\quad\phi_{1}(y,\tau_{0})=0\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+},

where

\displaystyle g_{1}=g_{1}(y,\tau):=g+c(\tau)\Delta\tilde{Z}_{0}(y)-c^{\prime}(\tau)\tilde{Z}_{0}(y),

\displaystyle h_{1}=h_{1}(\tilde{y},\tau):=h+c(\tau)\frac{n}{n-2}\left(U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\tilde{Z}_{0}\right)(\tilde{y},0)+c(\tau)\left({\partial}_{y_{n}}\tilde{Z}_{0}\right)(\tilde{y},0).

For
j=0,1,\dots,n
, by (
2.2
), (
2.3
), (
1.20
), orthogonality conditions in (
1.21
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}g_{1}(y,\tau)Z_{j}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}h_{1}(\tilde{y},\tau)Z_{j}(\tilde{y},0)d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}g(y,\tau)Z_{j}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}h(\tilde{y},\tau)Z_{j}(\tilde{y},0)d\tilde{y}+c(\tau)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\tilde{Z}_{0}(\tilde{y},0)\left({\partial}_{y_{n}}Z_{j}+\frac{n}{n-2}U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}Z_{j}\right)(\tilde{y},0)d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle+c(\tau)\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\tilde{Z}_{0}\Delta Z_{j}dy-c^{\prime}(\tau)\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\tilde{Z}_{0}Z_{j}dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{j0}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}g(y,\tau)Z_{0}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}h(\tilde{y},\tau)Z_{0}(\tilde{y},0)d\tilde{y}-\lambda_{0}c(\tau)\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\tilde{Z}_{0}Z_{0}dy-c^{\prime}(\tau)\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\tilde{Z}_{0}Z_{0}dy\right)

with
\lambda_{0}<0
. Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\tilde{Z}_{0}Z_{0}dy\neq 0
, we can take

c(\tau)=-\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\tilde{Z}_{0}Z_{0}dy\bigg)^{-1}e^{-\lambda_{0}\tau}\int_{\tau}^{\tau_{1}}e^{\lambda_{0}s}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}g(y,s)Z_{0}(y)dy+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}h(\tilde{y},s)Z_{0}(\tilde{y},0)d\tilde{y}\bigg)ds,

which makes
g_{1},h_{1}
satisfy (
4.4
).
Since
Z_{0}(y)
decays exponentially, by (
B.1
), we have

|c(\tau)|+|c^{\prime}(\tau)|\lesssim\tau^{\sigma}\left(\|g\|_{\sigma,2+a,\ell(\tau),\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}+\|h\|_{\sigma,1+a,\ell(\tau),\mathbb{R}^{n-1},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}\right).

Combining
\tilde{Z}_{0}(y)\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})\cap C^{1,\varsigma}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}})
,
\tilde{Z}_{0}(y)=0
for

|y|\geq C_{0}
, for
C_{1}=C_{\ell}\max\left\{C_{0},1\right\}
, we have

\displaystyle\|g_{1}\|_{\sigma,2+a,C_{1}\ell(\tau),\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}+\|h_{1}\|_{\sigma,1+a,C_{1}\ell(\tau),\mathbb{R}^{n-1},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\sigma,2+a,\ell(\tau),\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}+\|h\|_{\sigma,1+a,\ell(\tau),\mathbb{R}^{n-1},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}},

\displaystyle\|h_{1}\|_{\sigma,1+a,C_{1}\ell(\tau),\varsigma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\sigma,2+a,\ell(\tau),\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}+\|h\|_{\sigma,1+a,\ell(\tau),\varsigma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}.

Thus, we can use Proposition
4.2
to find
\phi_{1}
satisfying (
4.5
), (
4.6
). Set
C_{\phi}=c(\tau_{0})
. Combining (
1.21
), we complete the proof. ∎

Proof of Proposition
4.2
.

By the contraction mapping theorem, it is easy to get the existence and uniqueness for (
4.2
) in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times(\tau_{0},\tilde{\tau}))
for all
\tilde{\tau}\in(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\cap(\tau_{0},\infty)
and (
4.1
) holds in the weak sense. By the parabolic regularity theory,
\phi\in C(\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\times[\tau_{0},\tilde{\tau}))
.

We will use the comparison theorem for parabolic equations with the oblique derivative.
See

[
6
, p.122, 13.5 Theorem]
for instance.
Set
\tilde{\phi}(y,\tau)=e^{-\kappa\frac{|\tilde{y}|^{2}+\left(y_{n}+1\right)^{2}}{\tau}}
.
Using the supports of
g,h
, we have

\displaystyle\left({\partial}_{\tau}-\Delta\right)\tilde{\phi}-g=\tau^{-2}\left\{2\kappa n\tau+\kappa\left(1-4\kappa\right)\left[|\tilde{y}|^{2}+\left(y_{n}+1\right)^{2}\right]\right\}\tilde{\phi}\quad\mbox{ \ in \ }|y|>\ell(\tau),

\displaystyle\left(-{\partial}_{y_{n}}-\frac{n}{n-2}U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\right)\tilde{\phi}-h=\left(2\kappa\tau^{-1}-\frac{n}{n-2}U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\right)\tilde{\phi}\quad\mbox{ \ on \ }|\tilde{y}|>\ell(\tau),y_{n}=0.

\kappa\in(0,\frac{1}{4}]
is a necessary condition to make
C_{1}\tilde{\phi}
be a barrier function. We take
\kappa=\frac{1}{4}
.
For any
\tilde{\tau}\in(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\cap(\tau_{0},\infty)
,
C_{1}\tilde{\phi}
is a barrier function in
\left\{(y,\tau)\ |\ |y|\geq C_{2}\tau^{\max\left\{\frac{1}{2},p\right\}},\ \tau\in(\tau_{0},\tilde{\tau})\right\}
with sufficiently large constants
C_{1}
depending on
\tilde{\tau}
and
C_{2}>C_{\ell}
. By
\|g\|_{\sigma,2+a,\ell(\tau),\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}<\infty,\|h\|_{\sigma,1+a,\ell(\tau),\varsigma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}<\infty
, and (
2.29
) in Lemma
2.15
,
\nabla\phi(\cdot,\tau)
has exponential decay in space.

For
j=0,1,\dots,n
, we test (
4.1
) with
Z_{j}(y)\eta(M^{-1}y)
and integrate by parts,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\phi(y,\tau)Z_{j}(y)\eta(M^{-1}y)dy-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\phi(y,\tau_{0})Z_{j}(y)\eta(M^{-1}y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left(-{\partial}_{y_{n}}\phi Z_{j}\eta(M^{-1}\cdot)\right)((\tilde{y},0),s)d\tilde{y}ds+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left[\phi{\partial}_{y_{n}}\left(Z_{j}\eta(M^{-1}\cdot)\right)\right]((\tilde{y},0),s)d\tilde{y}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\phi\Delta\left(Z_{j}(y)\eta(M^{-1}y)\right)dyds+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}gZ_{j}(y)\eta(M^{-1}y)dyds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left[\left(\frac{n}{n-2}U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\phi+h\right)Z_{j}\eta(M^{-1}\cdot)\right]((\tilde{y},0),s)d\tilde{y}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left[\phi Z_{j}{\partial}_{y_{n}}\left(\eta(M^{-1}\cdot)\right)+\phi\eta(M^{-1}\cdot){\partial}_{y_{n}}Z_{j}\right]((\tilde{y},0),s)d\tilde{y}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\phi\left[\eta(M^{-1}y)\Delta Z_{j}(y)+2\nabla Z_{j}(y)\cdot\nabla\left(\eta(M^{-1}y)\right)+Z_{j}(y)\Delta\left(\eta(M^{-1}y)\right)\right]dyds

\displaystyle+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}gZ_{j}(y)\eta(M^{-1}y)dyds.

Taking
M\to\infty
gives

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\phi(y,\tau)Z_{j}(y)dy-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\phi(y,\tau_{0})Z_{j}(y)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left(\frac{n}{n-2}U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\phi Z_{j}+hZ_{j}+\phi{\partial}_{y_{n}}Z_{j}\right)((\tilde{y},0),s)d\tilde{y}ds+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\phi\Delta Z_{j}(y)dyds+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}gZ_{j}(y)dyds.

Using (
2.2
), (
2.3
), (
4.4
), and
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\phi(y,\tau_{0})Z_{j}(y)dy=0
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\phi(y,\tau)Z_{j}(y)dy=-\delta_{j0}\lambda_{0}\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\phi Z_{0}dy,

which implies (
4.5
).
Define

\|f\|_{\sigma,a,p,\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}^{\#}:=\inf\left\{C\ |\ |f(y,\tau)|\leq Cw(y,\tau)\mbox{ \ for \ }y\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n},\tau_{0}<\tau<\tau_{1}\right\},

where

w(y,\tau):=\tau^{\sigma}\langle y\rangle^{-a}\mathbf{1}_{|y|\leq\tau^{p}}+\tau^{\sigma-pa}e^{-\iota\frac{|y|^{2}}{\tau}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|>\tau^{p}},\quad\iota\in(0,\frac{1}{4}].

The weight
w(y,\tau)
is partially determined by the forthcoming estimate (
4.11
), (
4.13
). By the barrier function
C_{1}\tilde{\phi}
, for
1\leq\tau_{0}<\tau_{1}<\infty
, we have
e^{-\frac{|\tilde{y}|^{2}+\left(y_{n}+1\right)^{2}}{4\tau}}\leq C_{3}w(y,\tau)
for
y\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}
,
\tau_{0}<\tau<\tau_{1}
with a constant
C_{3}>0
depending on
\tau_{1}
, which implies

\|\phi\|_{\sigma,a,p,\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}^{\#}<\infty.

Claim: For all
1\leq\tau_{0}<\tau_{1}<\infty
, there exists a constant
C_{5}>0
independent of
\tau_{0},\tau_{1},g,h
such that

(4.7)

\|\phi\|_{\sigma,a,p,\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}^{\#}\leq C_{5}\left(\|g\|_{\sigma,2+a,\ell(\tau),\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}+\|h\|_{\sigma,1+a,\ell(\tau),\mathbb{R}^{n-1},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}\right).

Indeed, for
p\in[0,\frac{1}{2}]
, one taking
\tau_{1}\rightarrow\infty
, (
4.7
) deduces the estimate of
\phi
in (
4.6
) for the case
\tau_{1}=\infty
. Combining
\|g\|_{\sigma,2+a,\ell(\tau),\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}<\infty,\|h\|_{\sigma,1+a,\ell(\tau),\varsigma,\mathbb{R}^{n-1},\tau_{0},\tau_{1}}<\infty
, and (
2.29
) in Lemma
2.15
, we get the desired bound of
\nabla\phi
in (
4.6
).

We prove (
4.7
) by contradiction argument. Suppose that there exists a sequence
\left(\phi_{k},g_{k},h_{k},\tau_{0k},\tau_{1k}\right)_{k}
satisfying

(4.8)

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{\tau}\phi_{k}=\Delta\phi_{k}+g_{k}\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(\tau_{0k},\tau_{1k}),\quad-{\partial}_{y_{n}}\phi_{k}=\frac{n}{n-2}U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\phi_{k}+h_{k}\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(\tau_{0k},\tau_{1k}),\\
\phi_{k}(y,\tau_{0k})=0\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+},\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\phi_{k}(y,\tau)Z_{j}(y)dy=0\mbox{ \ for all \ }\tau\in(\tau_{0k},\tau_{1k}),\quad j=0,1,\dots,n,\end{cases}

and

(4.9)

\|\phi_{k}\|_{\sigma,a,p,\tau_{0k},\tau_{1k}}^{\#}=1,\quad\|g_{k}\|_{\sigma,2+a,\ell(\tau),\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n},\tau_{0k},\tau_{1k}}=o_{k}(1),\quad\|h_{k}\|_{\sigma,1+a,\ell(\tau),\mathbb{R}^{n-1},\tau_{0k},\tau_{1k}}=o_{k}(1),

where
o_{k}(1)\to 0

as
k\to\infty
. Therein,

\tau_{1k}\to\infty
otherwise
\|\phi_{k}\|_{\sigma,a,p,\tau_{0k},\tau_{1k}}^{\#}\to 0
.
Thus, there exists a sequence
\left(y_{k},\tau_{2k}\right)_{k}
,
y_{k}\in\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}
,
\tau_{0k}<\tau_{2k}<\tau_{1k}
such that

(4.10)

\left(w\left(y_{k},\tau_{2k}\right)\right)^{-1}\left|\phi_{k}(y_{k},\tau_{2k})\right|\geq\delta_{1}>0

with a small constant
\delta_{1}>0
independent of
k
, where
\tau_{2k}\rightarrow\infty
otherwise (
4.10
) fails.

For
(y,\tau)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[\tau_{0k},\tau_{1k})
, denote

\mathcal{T}_{1}\left[f_{1}\right]:=\int_{\tau_{0k}}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}G_{n}(y,\tau,z,s)f_{1}(z,s)dzds,\quad\mathcal{T}_{2}\left[f_{2}\right]:=\int_{\tau_{0k}}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}G_{n}(y,\tau,(\tilde{z},0),s)f_{2}(\tilde{z},s)d\tilde{z}ds

for some admissible functions
f_{1},f_{2}
. Then

\phi_{k}(y,\tau)=\mathcal{T}_{1}\left[g_{k}\right]+\mathcal{T}_{2}\left[\frac{n}{n-2}\left(U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\phi_{k}\right)((\tilde{y},0),\tau)+h_{k}\right].

Hereafter, all general constants, like
C_{i}
,
C(*,*,\dots)
, are independent of
k
.

By (
4.9
), Lemma
B.5
,

(4.11)

\left|\mathcal{T}_{1}\left[g_{k}\right]\right|\leq C_{6}o_{k}(1)\mathcal{T}_{1}\left[\tau^{\sigma}\langle y\rangle^{-2-a}\mathbf{1}_{|y|\leq\ell(\tau)}\right]\leq C_{6}o_{k}(1)\tau^{\sigma}\left(\langle y\rangle^{-a}\mathbf{1}_{|y|\leq\tau^{p}}+\tau^{-pa}e^{-\frac{|y|^{2}}{4\tau}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|>\tau^{p}}\right)

under the assumption

(4.12)

p\geq 0,\quad 0<a<n-2,\quad\begin{cases}p\leq\frac{1}{2},\sigma-pa+\frac{n}{2}\geq 0,&\mbox{ \ if \ }\sigma+p(n-2-a)\neq-1\\
p<\frac{1}{2}\left(\Leftrightarrow\sigma-pa+\frac{n}{2}>0\right),&\mbox{ \ if \ }\sigma+p(n-2-a)=-1,\end{cases}

where
C_{6}
varies from line to line.

By (
4.9
), Lemma
B.3
,

(4.13)

\left|\mathcal{T}_{2}\left[h_{k}\right]\right|\leq C_{6}o_{k}(1)\mathcal{T}_{2}\left[\tau^{\sigma}\langle\tilde{y}\rangle^{-1-a}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{y}|\leq\ell(\tau)}\right]\leq C_{6}o_{k}(1)\tau^{\sigma}\left(\langle y\rangle^{-a}\mathbf{1}_{|y|\leq\tau^{p}}+\tau^{-pa}e^{-\frac{|y|^{2}}{4\tau}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|>\tau^{p}}\right)

under the assumption (
4.12
).

By (
4.9
), Lemma
B.3
, Lemma
B.4
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{T}_{2}\left[\left(U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\phi_{k}\right)((\tilde{y},0),\tau)\right]\right|\leq C_{6}\mathcal{T}_{2}\left[\tau^{\sigma}\langle\tilde{y}\rangle^{-a-2}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{y}|\leq\tau^{p}}+\tau^{\sigma-pa}\langle\tilde{y}\rangle^{-2}e^{-\iota\frac{|\tilde{y}|^{2}}{\tau}}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{y}|>\tau^{p}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{6}\mathcal{T}_{2}\left[\tau^{\sigma}\langle\tilde{y}\rangle^{-a-1-\epsilon}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{y}|\leq\tau^{p}}+\tau^{\sigma-pa}|\tilde{y}|^{-1-\epsilon}\mathbf{1}_{\tau^{p}<|\tilde{y}|\leq\tau^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\tau^{\sigma-pa}|\tilde{y}|^{-1-\epsilon}e^{-\iota\frac{|\tilde{y}|^{2}}{\tau}}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{y}|>\tau^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{6}\bigg[\tau^{\sigma}\left(\langle y\rangle^{-a-\epsilon}\mathbf{1}_{|y|\leq\tau^{p}}+\tau^{-p(a+\epsilon)}e^{-\frac{|y|^{2}}{4\tau}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|>\tau^{p}}\right)

\displaystyle+\tau^{\sigma-pa}\left(\tau^{-p\epsilon}\mathbf{1}_{|y|\leq\tau^{p}}+|y|^{-\epsilon}\mathbf{1}_{\tau^{p}<|y|\leq\tau^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\tau^{-\frac{\epsilon}{2}}e^{-\frac{|y|^{2}}{4\tau}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|>\tau^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)+\tau^{\sigma-pa-\frac{\epsilon}{2}}e^{-\iota\frac{|y|^{2}}{\tau}}\bigg]

for any constant
\epsilon\in\left(0,\min\left\{n-2-a,1\right\}\right)
, under the assumption

\displaystyle 0<a<n-2,\quad p\geq 0,\quad\begin{cases}p\leq\frac{1}{2},\sigma-p(a+\epsilon)+\frac{n}{2}\geq 0,&\mbox{ \ if \ }\sigma+p(n-2-a-\epsilon)\neq-1\\
p<\frac{1}{2},&\mbox{ \ if \ }\sigma+p(n-2-a-\epsilon)=-1,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\iota\in(0,\frac{1}{4}),\quad\sigma-pa-\frac{\epsilon}{2}+2\iota n\geq 0.

Thus,

(4.14)

\left|\mathcal{T}_{2}\left[\left(U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\phi_{k}\right)((\tilde{y},0),\tau)\right]\right|\leq C_{6}\tau^{\sigma}\left(\langle y\rangle^{-a-\epsilon}\mathbf{1}_{|y|\leq\tau^{p}}+\tau^{-p(a+\epsilon)}e^{-\iota\frac{|y|^{2}}{\tau}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|>\tau^{p}}\right).

(
4.11
), (
4.13
), (
4.14
) imply

\left|\phi_{k}(y,\tau)\right|\leq C_{6}w(y,\tau)\left(o_{k}(1)+\langle y\rangle^{-\epsilon}\mathbf{1}_{|y|\leq\tau^{p}}+\tau^{-p\epsilon}\mathbf{1}_{|y|>\tau^{p}}\right).

Using
p>0
,
\tau_{2k}\rightarrow\infty
, and (
4.10
), we conclude that there exists a constant
C_{7}>0
such that
|y_{k}|\leq C_{7}
for all
k
.
By (
4.10
), for
k
large,
there exists a positive constant
C_{8}
such that

(4.15)

\tau_{2k}^{-\sigma}\left|\phi_{k}(y_{k},\tau_{2k})\right|\geq C_{8}>0.

Set

\tilde{\phi}_{k}(y,t)=\tau_{2k}^{-\sigma}\phi_{k}(y,\tau_{2k}+t),\quad\tilde{g}_{k}(y,t)=\tau_{2k}^{-\sigma}g_{k}(y,\tau_{2k}+t),\quad\tilde{h}_{k}(\tilde{y},t)=\tau_{2k}^{-\sigma}h_{k}(\tilde{y},\tau_{2k}+t).

Then,

(4.16)

|\tilde{\phi}_{k}(y_{k},0)|\geq C_{8}>0.

The following argument is in the same spirit as the proof of
[
11
, Lemma 7.7]
.

Case 1:
There exists a subsequence, (without loss of generality, we still use
k
as the serial number), such that
\tau_{2k}\geq 9\tau_{0k}
.
By (
4.8
), we have

(4.17)

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{t}\tilde{\phi}_{k}=\Delta\tilde{\phi}_{k}+\tilde{g}_{k}\quad\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(\tau_{0k}-\frac{\tau_{2k}}{2},0],\quad-{\partial}_{y_{n}}\tilde{\phi}_{k}=\frac{n}{n-2}U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\tilde{\phi}_{k}+\tilde{h}_{k}\quad\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(\tau_{0k}-\frac{\tau_{2k}}{2},0],\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\tilde{\phi}_{k}(y,t)Z_{j}(y)dy=0\quad\mbox{ \ for all \ }t\in(\tau_{0k}-\frac{\tau_{2k}}{2},0],\quad j=0,1,\dots,n.\end{cases}

And (
4.9
) implies

(4.18)

\displaystyle|\tilde{\phi}_{k}(y,t)|\leq C(\sigma,a,p,\iota)\left(\langle y\rangle^{-a}\mathbf{1}_{|y|<\tau_{2k}^{p}}+\tau_{2k}^{-pa}e^{-\iota\frac{|y|^{2}}{\tau_{2k}}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|\geq\tau_{2k}^{p}}\right),

\displaystyle|\tilde{g}_{k}(y,t)|\leq o_{k}(1)C(\sigma)\langle y\rangle^{-2-a}\mathbf{1}_{|y|\leq C_{\ell}\tau_{2k}^{p}},\quad|\tilde{h}_{k}(\tilde{y},t)|\leq o_{k}(1)C(\sigma)\langle\tilde{y}\rangle^{-1-a}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{y}|\leq C_{\ell}\tau_{2k}^{p}}

holding in
t\in(\tau_{0k}-\frac{\tau_{2k}}{2},0]
,
y\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}
,
\tilde{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
. Since
\tau_{2k}\geq 9\tau_{0k}
,
\tau_{2k}\to\infty
implies
\tau_{0k}-\frac{\tau_{2k}}{2}\to-\infty
.

Case 2:

\tau_{0k}<\tau_{2k}<9\tau_{0k}
holds for all
k
.
Similarly, by (
4.8
), we have

(4.19)

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{t}\tilde{\phi}_{k}=\Delta\tilde{\phi}_{k}+\tilde{g}_{k}\quad\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(\tau_{0k}-\tau_{2k},0],\quad-{\partial}_{y_{n}}\tilde{\phi}_{k}=\frac{n}{n-2}U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\tilde{\phi}_{k}+\tilde{h}_{k}\quad\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(\tau_{0k}-\tau_{2k},0],\\
\tilde{\phi}_{k}(y,\tau_{0k}-\tau_{2k})=0\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n},\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\tilde{\phi}_{k}(y,t)Z_{j}(y)dy=0\quad\mbox{ \ for all \ }t\in(\tau_{0k}-\tau_{2k},0],\quad j=0,1,\dots,n\end{cases}

and (
4.9
) makes (
4.18
) hold for all
t\in(\tau_{0k}-\tau_{2k},0]
,
y\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}
,
\tilde{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
. (
4.16
) implies
\tau_{0k}-\tau_{2k}\to-\infty
.

We will handle the above two cases in a unified way. Denote
t_{k}:=\begin{cases}\tau_{0k}-\frac{\tau_{2k}}{2},&\mbox{ \ for Case 1}\\
\tau_{0k}-\tau_{2k},&\mbox{ \ for Case 2}\end{cases}
.
Since
t_{k}\to-\infty
,
by (
4.18
) and the parabolic regularity theorem (see
[
37
, p.2418]
), up to a subsequence, we have

(4.20)

\tilde{\phi}_{k}\to\tilde{\phi}\mbox{ \ in \ }C_{\mathrm{loc}}^{\alpha,\frac{\alpha}{2}}\left(\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\times(-\infty,0]\right)\mbox{ \ with a constant \ }\alpha\in(0,1).

Combining (
4.16
) with
|y_{k}|\leq C_{7}
, (
4.18
), we have

(4.21)

\tilde{\phi}\not\equiv 0,\mbox{ \ and \ }|\tilde{\phi}(y,t)|\leq C(\sigma,a,p,\iota)\langle y\rangle^{-a}\mbox{ \ for \ }(y,t)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}\times(-\infty,0].

Given
t\in(-\infty,0]
,
for any
\epsilon_{1}>0
, taking a large constant
R_{1}
, then for all
j=0,1,\dots,n
, when
k
is sufficiently large, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}\cap\{|y|\geq R_{1}\}}\tilde{\phi}_{k}(y,t)Z_{j}(y)dy\bigg|\leq C(\sigma,a,p,\iota,n)\int_{|y|\geq R_{1}}\left(|y|^{2-n-a}\mathbf{1}_{|y|<\tau_{2k}^{p}}+|y|^{2-n}\tau_{2k}^{-pa}e^{-\iota\frac{|y|^{2}}{\tau_{2k}}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|\geq\tau_{2k}^{p}}\right)dy

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\sigma,a,p,\iota,n)\bigg(R_{1}^{2-a}+\tau_{2k}^{1-pa}\int_{\tau_{2k}^{2p-1}}^{\infty}e^{-\iota z}dz\bigg)<\epsilon_{1},

where in the last step, we require the assumption

a>2,\quad pa>1.

Similarly, for
a>2
, we have

\big|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}\cap\{|y|\geq R_{1}\}}\tilde{\phi}(y,t)Z_{j}(y)dy\big|<\epsilon_{1}
.
By (
4.20
), we have

\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}\cap\{|y|<R_{1}\}}\tilde{\phi}_{k}(y,t)Z_{j}(y)dy=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}\cap\{|y|<R_{1}\}}\tilde{\phi}(y,t)Z_{j}(y)dy.

Thus, the orthogonality conditions in (
4.17
), (
4.19
) yields

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\tilde{\phi}(y,t)Z_{j}(y)dy=0\quad\text{ for all }t\in(-\infty,0],\quad j=0,1,\dots,n.

By (
4.17
), (
4.19
), we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{k}(y,t)=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}G_{n}(y,t,z,t_{k})\tilde{\phi}_{k}(z,t_{k})dz+\int_{t_{k}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}G_{n}(y,t,z,s)\tilde{g}_{k}(z,s)dzds

\displaystyle+\int_{t_{k}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}G_{n}(y,t,(\tilde{z},0),s)\left[\frac{n}{n-2}\left(U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\tilde{\phi}_{k}\right)((\tilde{z},0),s)+\tilde{h}_{k}(\tilde{z},s)\right]d\tilde{z}ds.

Taking
k\to\infty
, using (
4.18
),
a>0
,
[
59
, Lemma A.3]
for the first part in Case 1,
\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(4\pi t\right)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{4t}}dt=|x|^{2-n}\pi^{-\frac{n}{2}}2^{-1}(n-2)^{-1}\Gamma(\frac{n}{2})
with
n>2
for the second and third parts,
we have

(4.22)

\tilde{\phi}(y,t)=\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}G_{n}(y,t,(\tilde{z},0),s)\frac{n}{n-2}\left(U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\tilde{\phi}\right)((\tilde{z},0),s)d\tilde{z}ds,

which satisfies

(4.23)

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{t}\tilde{\phi}=\Delta\tilde{\phi}\quad\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(-\infty,0],\quad-{\partial}_{y_{n}}\tilde{\phi}=\frac{n}{n-2}U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\tilde{\phi}\quad\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(-\infty,0],\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\tilde{\phi}(y,t)Z_{j}(y)dy=0\quad\mbox{ \ for all \ }t\in(-\infty,0],\quad j=0,1,\dots,n.\end{cases}

Using (
4.22
), there exists a constant
C_{9}
varying from line to line , such that

(4.24)

|\tilde{\phi}|\leq C_{9}\langle y\rangle^{2-n},

and
\tilde{\phi}
is smooth by the parabolic regularity theory (See
[
34
,
37
]
). By the scaling argument, we have

(4.25)

\langle y\rangle^{-1}|D\tilde{\phi}|+|\tilde{\phi}_{t}|+|D^{2}\tilde{\phi}|\leq C_{9}\langle y\rangle^{-n}.

Differentiating (
4.23
), we get

(4.26)

\begin{cases}{\partial}_{t}\tilde{\phi}_{t}=\Delta\tilde{\phi}_{t}\quad\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(-\infty,0],\quad-{\partial}_{y_{n}}\tilde{\phi}_{t}=\frac{n}{n-2}U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}\tilde{\phi}_{t}\quad\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times(-\infty,0],\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\tilde{\phi}_{t}(y,t)Z_{j}(y)dy=0\quad\mbox{ \ for all \ }t\in(-\infty,0],\quad j=0,1,\dots,n\end{cases}

and then the scaling argument gives

\langle y\rangle^{-1}|D\tilde{\phi}_{t}|+|\tilde{\phi}_{tt}|+|D^{2}\tilde{\phi}_{t}|\leq C_{9}\langle y\rangle^{-n-2}.

Moreover, multiplying (
4.26
) by
\tilde{\phi}_{t}
and integrating by parts, we get

\frac{1}{2}{\partial}_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|\tilde{\phi}_{t}|^{2}dy+B[\tilde{\phi}_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}]=0,

where

B[u,v]:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\nabla u\cdot\nabla vdy-\frac{n}{n-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left(U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}uv\right)(\tilde{y},0)d\tilde{y}
.

Then
B[\tilde{\phi}_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}]\geq 0
by
\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\tilde{\phi}_{t}(y,t)Z_{0}(y)dy=0
since
Z_{0}
is the only eigenfucntion of (
2.3
) with negative eigenvalue. Thus,
\partial_{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}|\tilde{\phi}_{t}|^{2}dy\leq 0
.

Multiplying (
4.23
) by
\tilde{\phi}_{t}
and integrating by parts, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}|\tilde{\phi}_{t}|^{2}dy=-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}B[\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\phi}].

By (
4.24
), (
4.25
), for
n>2
,
|B[\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\phi}](t)|
is uniformly bounded for
t\in(-\infty,0]
. Thus, we have

\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}|\tilde{\phi}_{t}|^{2}dydt<\infty.

Hence
\tilde{\phi}_{t}=0
, that is,
\tilde{\phi}=\tilde{\phi}(y)
is independent of
t
.

By (
4.23
) and Lemma
4.1
, we have
\tilde{\phi}\equiv 0
, which contradicts
\tilde{\phi}\not\equiv 0
in (
4.21
).
As a result, (
4.7
) holds.
Due to the arbitrarily small choice of
\epsilon
, we conclude the parameters restriction (
4.3
).
∎

5.
Leading term of
\mu_{i}
and topology of the inner and outer problems

Hereafter, we focus on the case
n=5
unless otherwise specified.

In order to apply Proposition
1.2
to solve the inner problems (
3.4
), suitable
\bm{\mu},\bm{\xi}
will be taken to satisfy the orthogonality conditions (
1.20
).

Recall (
1.14
).
\Theta_{l_{i}}(0,t)=-(T-t)^{l_{i}}
is a leading role in the orthogonal equations. Roughly speaking,
\psi
is a smaller term compared with
\Theta_{l_{i}}(0,t)
in the inner problem. As the leading term of
\mu_{i}
,
\mu_{i,0}
is determined by

(5.1)

\displaystyle\dot{\mu}_{i,0}\mu_{i,0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}Z_{n}^{2}(y)\eta\Big(\frac{y}{4R}\Big)dy+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\frac{n}{n-2}\mu_{i,0}^{\frac{n}{2}-1}U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}(\tilde{y},0)\eta\Big(\frac{\tilde{y}}{4R},0\Big)\Theta_{l_{i}}(0,t)Z_{n}(\tilde{y},0)d\tilde{y}=0

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\dot{\mu}_{i,0}\mu_{i,0}^{2-\frac{n}{2}}=-\left(T-t\right)^{l_{i}}A_{R}

with

(5.2)

\displaystyle A_{R}:=-\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}Z_{n}^{2}(y)\eta\Big(\frac{y}{4R}\Big)dy\Big)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\frac{n}{n-2}U^{\frac{2}{n-2}}(\tilde{y},0)\eta\Big(\frac{\tilde{y}}{4R},0\Big)Z_{n}(\tilde{y},0)d\tilde{y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n-2}{2}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}Z_{n}^{2}(y)dy\Big)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}U^{\frac{n}{n-2}}(\tilde{y},0)d\tilde{y}+O(R^{-1}),

where we used (
2.1
) and
n=5
for the last step of
A_{R}
.
We take

(5.3)

\mu_{i,0}(t)=\left[A_{R}\frac{6-n}{2}\left(l_{i}+1\right)^{-1}\right]^{\frac{2}{6-n}}\left(T-t\right)^{\frac{2}{6-n}\left(l_{i}+1\right)}.

Let
\mu_{i,1}(t):=\mu_{i}(t)-\mu_{i,0}(t)
be the minor term of
\mu_{i}(t)
. Denote

\bm{\mu}_{,0}=(\mu_{1,0},\mu_{2,0},\dots,\mu_{\mathfrak{o},0}),\quad\bm{\mu}_{,1}=(\mu_{1,1},\mu_{2,1},\dots,\mu_{\mathfrak{o},1}).

For
T\ll 1
, one plugging
n=5
, then there exists a constant
C_{\mu_{i,0}}>9
sufficiently large such that

(5.4)

9C_{\mu_{i,0}}^{-1}\left(T-t\right)^{2l_{i}+2}\leq\mu_{i,0}(t)\leq 9^{-1}C_{\mu_{i,0}}\left(T-t\right)^{2l_{i}+2},\quad\left|\dot{\mu}_{i,0}(t)\right|\leq 9^{-1}C_{\mu_{i,0}}\left(T-t\right)^{2l_{i}+1}.

We make the ansatz that

(5.5)

\displaystyle C_{\mu_{i,0}}^{-1}\left(T-t\right)^{2l_{i}+2}\leq\mu_{i}\leq C_{\mu_{i,0}}(T-t)^{2l_{i}+2},\quad|\dot{\mu}_{i}|\leq C_{\mu_{i,0}}(T-t)^{2l_{i}+1},

\displaystyle\left|\xi^{[i]}-\tilde{q}^{[i]}\right|\leq C_{\mu_{i,0}}(T-t)^{2l_{i}+2},\quad|\dot{\xi}^{[i]}|\leq C_{\mu_{i,0}}(T-t)^{2l_{i}+1}.

Under the ansatz (
5.5
),

\left|\dot{\mu}_{i}\mu_{i}Z_{5}(y^{[i]})\right|\eta(\frac{y^{[i]}}{4R})\lesssim\left(T-t\right)^{4l_{i}+3}\langle y^{[i]}\rangle^{-3}\eta(\frac{y^{[i]}}{4R})\lesssim R^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(T-t\right)^{4l_{i}+3}\langle y^{[i]}\rangle^{-\frac{9}{2}}\eta(\frac{y^{[i]}}{4R})
. Using Proposition
1.2
with
n=5
formally, for
i=1,2,\dots,\mathfrak{o}
, we define
T_{\sigma_{0}}:=T-\sigma_{0}
with
\sigma_{0}\in[0,T)
, and the spaces

(5.6)

B_{{\rm in},\sigma_{0}}:=\Big\{(f_{1},f_{2},\dots,f_{\mathfrak{o}})\ \big|\ f_{i},\nabla f_{i}\in L^{\infty}\left(B_{5}^{+}(0,2R)\times(0,T_{\sigma_{0}})\right),i=1,2,\dots,\mathfrak{o},\quad\sup_{i=1,2,\dots,\mathfrak{o}}\|f_{i}\|_{{\rm{in}},l_{i},\sigma_{0}}\leq 1\Big\}

with the norm

\|f\|_{{\rm{in}},l_{i},\sigma_{0}}:=\sup_{(y,t)\in B_{5}^{+}(0,2R)\times(0,T_{\sigma_{0}})}\big(R^{2}(T-t)^{4l_{i}+3}\langle y\rangle^{-\frac{5}{2}}\big)^{-1}\left(|f(y,t)|+\langle y\rangle|\nabla f(y,t)|\right),

where we used
R^{2}
instead of
R^{\frac{3}{2}}
in the norm
\|\cdot\|_{{\rm{in}},l_{i},\sigma_{0}}
for the final fixed point argument.

In order to get the vanishing property around the blow-up points for the outer problem, we adopt the ideas in
[
25
, p.318]
,
[
60
, p.13]
to define the following space for the outer problem

(5.7)

\mathcal{X}_{\delta_{0},\sigma_{0}}:=\left\{f\in L^{\infty}(\overline{B_{5}^{+}(0,\sigma_{0}^{-1})}\times(0,T_{\sigma_{0}}))\ |\ \|f\|_{\mathcal{X},\sigma_{0}}\leq\delta_{0}\right\}

with the norm

\|f\|_{\mathcal{X},\sigma_{0}}:=\sup_{(x,t)\in\overline{B_{5}^{+}(0,\sigma_{0}^{-1})}\times(0,T_{\sigma_{0}})}\Big[\langle x\rangle^{-2}\mathbf{1}_{\cap_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{o}}\big\{|z^{[i]}|>e^{\frac{l_{i}s}{2l_{i}+2}}\big\}}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{o}}(T-t)^{l_{i}}\langle z^{[i]}\rangle^{2l_{i}+2}\mathbf{1}_{|z^{[i]}|\leq e^{\frac{l_{i}s}{2l_{i}+2}}}\Big]^{-1}|f(x,t)|

with
z^{[i]}=\frac{x-q^{[i]}}{\sqrt{T-t}}
and
s=-\ln(T-t)
, where we admit
B_{5}^{+}(0,\sigma_{0}^{-1})\big|_{\sigma_{0}=0}=\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5}
and the constant
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
will be determined later.
Note that
|z^{[i]}|\leq e^{\frac{l_{i}s}{2l_{i}+2}}\Leftrightarrow|x-q^{[i]}|\leq(T-t)^{\frac{1}{2l_{i}+2}}
, which implies
\{|z^{[i]}|\leq e^{\frac{l_{i}s}{2l_{i}+2}}\}\cap\{|z^{[j]}|\leq e^{\frac{l_{j}s}{2l_{j}+2}}\}=\emptyset
for
i\neq j
with
T\ll 1
.
Given
q\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5}
,
\langle x\rangle\sim_{q}\langle x-q\rangle
.

6.
Priori estimate of the outer problem

Set
N_{\rm{max}}=\max\limits_{i=1,2,\dots,\mathfrak{o}}N(\lceil 5l_{i}/3\rceil+1)
with
N(\cdot)
defined in (
2.12
). We set
\tilde{e}_{j}(z)
by Corollary
2.5
satisfying the properties

(6.1)

\displaystyle\tilde{e}_{j}(z)=\sum\limits_{\iota=0}^{N_{\rm{max}}}a_{j\iota}e_{\iota}(z)\eta(\frac{z}{C_{\tilde{e}}})\mbox{ \ with a constant \ }C_{\tilde{e}}>0,\quad j=0,1,\dots,N_{\rm{max}},

\displaystyle\partial_{z_{n}}\tilde{e}_{i}=0\quad\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}\quad\mbox{ \ and \ }\quad\left(\tilde{e}_{i},e_{j}\right)_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}=\delta_{ij}\quad\mbox{ \ for \ }i,j=0,1,\dots,N_{\rm{max}}

with a constant matrix
(a_{il})_{(N_{\rm{max}}+1)\times(N_{\rm{max}}+1)}
. Denote

(6.2)

\bm{\tilde{e}}_{i}(z):=\left(\tilde{e}_{0}(z),\tilde{e}_{1}(z),\dots,\tilde{e}_{N(\lceil 5l_{i}/3\rceil+1)}(z)\right).

We emphasize the following remark before further analysis.

Remark 6.0.1
.

The property of
\bm{\mu}(\bm{\xi})
being the original function of
\dot{\bm{\mu}}(\dot{\bm{\xi}})
plays no role in this section, for which we can regard
\bm{\mu},\dot{\bm{\mu}},\bm{\xi},\dot{\bm{\xi}}
as four independent functions in this section.

In order to find a solution for the outer problem (
3.6
) with fast time decay near the blow-up points, we consider the following equation with a suitable initial value

(6.3)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}\psi=\Delta_{x}\psi+\mathcal{G}_{1}[\bm{\phi},\bm{\mu},\bm{\xi}]\text{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5}\times(0,T),\quad-\partial_{x_{5}}\psi=\mathcal{G}_{2}[\psi,\bm{\phi},\bm{\mu},\bm{\xi}]\text{ \ on \ }\partial\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5}\times(0,T),\\
&\psi(x,0)=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{o}}\bm{b}_{i}\cdot\bm{\tilde{e}}_{i}\big(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-q^{[i]})\big)+\varphi_{0}(x)\text{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5},\end{aligned}\right.

where
\varphi_{0}(x)\in C_{\rm c}^{\infty}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{5}_{+}})
and
\bm{b}_{i}=(b_{i,0},b_{i,1},\dots,b_{i,N(\lceil 5l_{i}/3\rceil+1)})\in\mathbb{R}^{N(\lceil 5l_{i}/3\rceil+1)}
will be determined later. By (
2.17
), (
6.3
) is rewritten as

(6.4)

\displaystyle\psi(x,t)=\mathcal{T}^{\rm{out}}[\psi,\bm{\phi},\bm{\mu},\bm{\xi}]:=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5}}G_{5}(x,t,z,s)\mathcal{G}_{1}[\bm{\phi},\bm{\mu},\bm{\xi}](z,s)dzds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}G_{5}(x,t,(\tilde{z},0),s)\mathcal{G}_{2}[\psi,\bm{\phi},\bm{\mu},\bm{\xi}]((\tilde{z},0),s)d\tilde{z}ds+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5}}G_{5}(x,t,z,0)\Big[\sum\limits_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{o}}\bm{b}_{i}\cdot\bm{\tilde{e}}_{i}\big(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-q^{[i]})\big)+\varphi_{0}(z)\Big]dz.

Adopting the idea in
[
60
, p.14]
about the distribution of the right-hand side,
we decompose
\psi=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{o}+1}\psi_{i}
, where
\psi_{i}
satisfy the following equations respectively. For
i=1,2,\dots,\mathfrak{o}
,

(6.5)

\partial_{t}\psi_{i}=\Delta_{x}\psi_{i}+\mathcal{G}_{1,i}\text{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5}\times(0,T),\quad-\partial_{x_{5}}\psi_{i}=\mathcal{G}_{2,i}\text{ \ on \ }\partial\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5}\times(0,T),\quad\psi_{i}(x,0)={\bm{b}}_{i}\cdot{\bm{\tilde{e}}}_{i}\big(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-q^{[i]})\big)\text{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5},

and for
i=\mathfrak{o}+1
,

(6.6)

\partial_{t}\psi_{\mathfrak{o}+1}=\Delta_{x}\psi_{\mathfrak{o}+1}\text{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5}\times(0,T),\quad-\partial_{x_{5}}\psi_{\mathfrak{o}+1}=\mathcal{G}_{2,\mathfrak{o}+1}\text{ \ on \ }\partial\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5}\times(0,T),\quad\psi_{\mathfrak{o}+1}(x,0)=\varphi_{0}(x)\text{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5},

where for
i=1,2,\dots,\mathfrak{o}
,

(6.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1,i}=\mathcal{G}_{1,i}[\phi_{i},\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}]:=\Lambda_{1,i}[\phi_{i},\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}]+\Lambda_{2,i}[\phi_{i},\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}]+\mathcal{E}_{1}\left[U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right]\left(\eta_{2\delta}(x^{[i]})-\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})\right)+\mathcal{E}_{U,i}^{\rm{cut}}+\mathcal{E}_{\Theta,i}^{\rm{cut}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{2,i}=\mathcal{G}_{2,i}\left[\psi,\bm{\phi},\bm{\mu},\bm{\xi}\right]:=\Big\{\eta_{4\delta}(x^{[i]})\mathcal{N}\left[\psi,\bm{\phi},\bm{\mu},\bm{\xi}\right]+\left(U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right)^{\frac{5}{3}}\left(\eta_{2\delta}^{\frac{5}{3}}(x^{[i]})-\eta_{2\delta}(x^{[i]})\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{5}{3}\left(U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\Big[\left(\eta_{\delta}(x^{[i]})-\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})\right)\Theta_{l_{i}}(x^{[i]},t)+\left(\eta_{2\delta}^{\frac{2}{3}}(x^{[i]})-\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})\right)\psi(x,t)\Big]\Big\}\Big|_{x_{5}=0},

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{2,\mathfrak{o}+1}=\mathcal{G}_{2,\mathfrak{o}+1}[\psi]:=\mathcal{G}_{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{o}}\mathcal{G}_{2,i}=\Big\{\Big(1-\sum_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{o}}\eta_{4\delta}(x^{[i]})\Big)\mathcal{N}\left[\psi,\bm{\phi},\bm{\mu},\bm{\xi}\right]\Big\}\Big|_{x_{5}=0}

\displaystyle=\Big\{\Big(1-\sum_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{o}}\eta_{4\delta}(x^{[i]})\Big)|\psi|^{\frac{2}{3}}\psi\Big\}\Big|_{x_{5}=0}.

Here we used (
5.5
) and
T\ll 1
for
\mathcal{G}_{2,\mathfrak{o}+1}
and

\left(\eta_{2\delta}^{\frac{2}{3}}(x^{[i]})-1\right)\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})\mu_{i}^{-\frac{3}{2}}\phi_{i}(y^{[i]},t)\equiv 0
for
\mathcal{G}_{2,i}
.

For
i=1,2,\dots,\mathfrak{o}
, set

(6.8)

\psi_{i}(x,t)=\Psi_{i}\big((T-t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(x-q^{[i]}\right),-\ln(T-t)\big),\quad\mbox{ that is, }\quad\Psi_{i}(z^{[i]},s)=\psi_{i}\big(e^{-\frac{s}{2}}z^{[i]}+q^{[i]},T-e^{-s}\big).

By Lemma
2.6
, (
6.5
) is rewritten as

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{s}\Psi_{i}=A_{z^{[i]}}\Psi_{i}+g_{1,i}(z^{[i]},s)\quad\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5}\times(s_{0},\infty),\quad-\partial_{z_{5}^{[i]}}\Psi_{i}=g_{2,i}(\tilde{z}^{[i]},s)\quad\mbox{ \ on \ }\partial\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5}\times(s_{0},\infty),\\
&\Psi_{i}\big(z^{[i]},s_{0}\big)=\bm{b}_{i}\cdot\bm{\tilde{e}}_{i}(z^{[i]})\quad\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5},\end{aligned}\right.

where

(6.9)

s_{0}:=-\ln T,\quad g_{1,i}(z^{[i]},s):=e^{-s}\mathcal{G}_{1,i}(e^{-\frac{s}{2}}z^{[i]}+q^{[i]},T-e^{-s}),\quad g_{2,i}(\tilde{z}^{[i]},s):=e^{-\frac{s}{2}}\mathcal{G}_{2,i}(e^{-\frac{s}{2}}\tilde{z}^{[i]}+\tilde{q}^{[i]},T-e^{-s}).

In order to find a solution
\Psi_{i}(z^{[i]},s)
of the form

(6.10)

\Psi_{i}(z^{[i]},s)=\bm{d}_{i}(s)\cdot\bm{\tilde{e}}_{i}(z^{[i]})+\Phi_{i}(z^{[i]},s)

with
\bm{d}_{i}(s)=(d_{i,0}(s),d_{i,1}(s),\dots,d_{i,N(\lceil 5l_{i}/3\rceil+1)}(s))\in C^{1}([s_{0},\infty))
to be determined later, one using
\partial_{z_{n}}\tilde{e}_{i}=0

on
{\partial}\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}
in (
6.1
), it suffices to solve

(6.11)

\partial_{s}\Phi_{i}=A_{z^{[i]}}\Phi_{i}+\tilde{g}_{1,i}(z^{[i]},s)\text{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5}\times(s_{0},\infty),\quad-\partial_{z^{[i]}_{5}}\Phi_{i}=g_{2,i}(\tilde{z}^{[i]},s)\text{ \ on \ }\partial\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5}\times(s_{0},\infty),\quad\Phi_{i}(\cdot,s_{0})=0\text{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5},

where we set

(6.12)

\bm{b}_{i}:=\bm{d}_{i}(s_{0}),\quad\tilde{g}_{1,i}(z^{[i]},s):=g_{1,i}(z^{[i]},s)+\sum_{j=0}^{N(\lceil 5l_{i}/3\rceil+1)}\left(d_{i,j}(s)A_{z^{[i]}}\tilde{e}_{j}(z^{[i]})-\dot{d}_{i,j}(s)\tilde{e}_{j}(z^{[i]})\right).

In order to recover the time decay of the right-hand sides of (
6.11
) for
\Phi_{i}
, we impose the orthogonality conditions

(6.13)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{5}_{+}}\tilde{g}_{1,i}(z^{[i]},s)e_{j}(z^{[i]})e^{-\frac{|z^{[i]}|^{2}}{4}}dz^{[i]}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}g_{2,i}(\tilde{z}^{[i]},s)e_{j}(\tilde{z}^{[i]},0)e^{-\frac{|\tilde{z}^{[i]}|^{2}}{4}}d\tilde{z}^{[i]}=0,\quad j=0,1,\dots,N(\lceil 5l_{i}/3\rceil+1).

One using (
2.6
), (
2.11
),
and (
6.1
),
then (
6.13
) is equivalent to

(6.14)

\dot{d}_{i,j}(s)+\lambda_{j}d_{i,j}(s)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{5}_{+}}g_{1,i}(z^{[i]},s)e_{j}(z^{[i]})e^{-\frac{|z^{[i]}|^{2}}{4}}dz^{[i]}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}g_{2,i}(\tilde{z}^{[i]},s)e_{j}(\tilde{z}^{[i]},0)e^{-\frac{|\tilde{z}^{[i]}|^{2}}{4}}d\tilde{z}^{[i]}

for
j=0,1,\dots,N(\lceil 5l_{i}/3\rceil+1)
.

6.1.
Estimates of
\mathcal{G}_{2,\mathfrak{o}+1}
,
\mathcal{G}_{1,i}
,
\mathcal{G}_{2,i}
,
i=1,2,\dots,\mathfrak{o}
in (
6.7
), and
d_{i,j}(s)

Lemma 6.1
.

Suppose that
\bm{\mu},\dot{\bm{\mu}},\bm{\xi},\dot{\bm{\xi}}
satisfy (
5.5
),
\psi\in\mathcal{X}_{\delta_{0},0}
, and
T\ll 1
, then

(6.15)

\left|\mathcal{G}_{2,\mathfrak{o}+1}\right|\leq\delta_{0}^{\frac{5}{3}}\langle\tilde{x}\rangle^{-\frac{10}{3}}\mathbf{1}_{\cap_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{o}}\{|\tilde{x}^{[i]}|\geq 4\delta\}}.

Under the additional assumption that
\bm{\phi}\in B_{{\rm in},0}
, then there exists a constant
C
independent of
T,\delta_{0}
such that for
i=1,2,\dots,\mathfrak{o}
,
\mathcal{G}_{1,i}
,
\mathcal{G}_{2,i}
have the pointwise upper bounds

(6.16)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{G}_{1,i}|\leq

\displaystyle C\Big\{R^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mu_{i}^{-2}e^{-l_{i}s}\left(\langle y^{[i]}\rangle^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{1}_{|y^{[i]}|\leq 2R}+\langle y^{[i]}\rangle^{-\frac{11}{4}}\mathbf{1}_{|y^{[i]}|>R}\right)\mathbf{1}_{|z^{[i]}|\leq 1}

\displaystyle+\left[e^{-(3l_{i}+\frac{1}{2})s}|z^{[i]}|^{-3}+\mathbf{1}_{\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}}\leq|z^{[i]}|\leq 2\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}}}\right]\mathbf{1}_{1<|z^{[i]}|\leq 4\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}}}\Big\},

(6.17)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{G}_{2,i}|\leq

\displaystyle C\Big[\left(R^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mu_{i}^{-1}e^{-l_{i}s}\langle\tilde{y}^{[i]}\rangle^{-\frac{7}{4}}+e^{-\frac{5}{3}l_{i}s}\right)\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{z}^{[i]}|\leq 1}+e^{-\frac{5}{3}l_{i}s}|\tilde{z}^{[i]}|^{\frac{10}{3}l_{i}+\frac{10}{3}}\mathbf{1}_{1<|\tilde{z}^{[i]}|\leq e^{\frac{l_{i}s}{2l_{i}+2}}}

\displaystyle+\left(e^{-\frac{5}{3}l_{i}s}|\tilde{z}^{[i]}|^{\frac{10}{3}l_{i}}+\delta_{0}^{\frac{5}{3}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{e^{\frac{l_{i}s}{2l_{i}+2}}<|\tilde{z}^{[i]}|\leq 8\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}}}\Big].

Proof.

The estimate of
\mathcal{G}_{2,\mathfrak{o}+1}
is straightforward.
By (
5.5
), we have

(6.18)

\langle y^{[i]}\rangle\sim 2C_{\mu_{i},0}^{2}+|y^{[i]}|\sim\langle\mu_{i}^{-1}x^{[i]}\rangle=\langle\mu_{i}^{-1}\left(T-t\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}z^{[i]}\rangle\sim\langle e^{(2l_{i}+\frac{3}{2})s}z^{[i]}\rangle.

Step 1: Estimate of
\mathcal{G}_{1,i}
.
Recall (
3.2
).
One using
R=|\ln{T}|
, (
5.5
),
\|\phi_{i}\|_{{\rm{in}},l_{i},0}\leq 1
(by
\bm{\phi}\in B_{{\rm in},0}
), then

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{1,i}+\Lambda_{2,i}|\lesssim\mu_{i}^{-2}\mu_{i}^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left(R^{-2}|\phi_{i}|+R^{-1}|\nabla_{y^{[i]}}\phi_{i}|+\mu_{i}|\dot{\mu}_{i}||\phi_{i}|+\mu_{i}R^{-1}|\dot{\xi}^{[i]}||\phi_{i}|\right)\mathbf{1}_{R\leq|y^{[i]}|\leq 2R}

\displaystyle+\mu_{i}^{-2}\left[|\dot{\mu}_{i}|\mu_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\left|\phi_{i}\right|+\left|y^{[i]}\cdot\nabla_{y^{[i]}}\phi_{i}\right|\right)+\mu_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\dot{\xi}^{[i]}||\nabla_{\tilde{y}^{[i]}}\phi_{i}|\right]\mathbf{1}_{|y^{[i]}|\leq 2R}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu_{i}^{-2}\left(T-t\right)^{l_{i}}R^{-\frac{5}{2}}\mathbf{1}_{R\leq|y^{[i]}|\leq 2R}+\mu_{i}^{-2}R^{2}\left(T-t\right)^{5l_{i}+3}\langle y^{[i]}\rangle^{-\frac{5}{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|y^{[i]}|\leq 2R}\lesssim R^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mu_{i}^{-2}e^{-l_{i}s}\langle y^{[i]}\rangle^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{1}_{|y^{[i]}|\leq 2R}.

Note that
|y^{[i]}|\leq 2R
implies
|z^{[i]}|\leq 1
.

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{E}_{1}\left[U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right]\left(\eta_{2\delta}(x^{[i]})-\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})\right)\right|\lesssim\left(\mathbf{1}_{|x^{[i]}|\leq 4\delta}-\mathbf{1}_{|y^{[i]}|\leq R}\right)\left(T-t\right)^{-3l_{i}-4}\langle y^{[i]}\rangle^{-3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{|x^{[i]}|\leq 4\delta,\ |y^{[i]}|>R}\left(T-t\right)^{-3l_{i}-4}\langle y^{[i]}\rangle^{-3}\lesssim\mu_{i}^{-2}e^{-l_{i}s}\langle y^{[i]}\rangle^{-3}\mathbf{1}_{|z^{[i]}|\leq 1,\ |y^{[i]}|>R}+e^{-(3l_{i}+\frac{1}{2})s}|z^{[i]}|^{-3}\mathbf{1}_{1<|z^{[i]}|\leq 4\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}}},

where we used (
6.18
) for the last “
\lesssim
”.

\left|\mathcal{E}_{U,i}^{\rm{cut}}\right|\lesssim\left(\mu_{i}^{-\frac{3}{2}}\langle y^{[i]}\rangle^{-3}+\mu_{i}^{-\frac{5}{2}}\langle y^{[i]}\rangle^{-4}\right)\mathbf{1}_{2\delta\leq|x^{[i]}|\leq 4\delta}\sim e^{-(3l_{i}+\frac{3}{2})s}|z^{[i]}|^{-3}\mathbf{1}_{2\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}}\leq|z^{[i]}|\leq 4\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}}},

where we used (
6.18
) for the last “
\sim
”. Using (
1.14
), then

\left|\mathcal{E}_{\Theta,i}^{\rm{cut}}\right|\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{\delta\leq|x^{[i]}|\leq 2\delta}=\mathbf{1}_{\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}}\leq|z^{[i]}|\leq 2\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}}}.

Summarizing all the estimates reaches the upper bound of
\mathcal{G}_{1,i}
.

Step2: Estimate of
\mathcal{G}_{2,i}
.
By (
6.18
),

\displaystyle\left|\left(U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right)^{\frac{5}{3}}\left(\eta_{2\delta}^{\frac{5}{3}}(x^{[i]})-\eta_{2\delta}(x^{[i]})\right)\right|\lesssim\mu_{i}^{-\frac{5}{2}}\langle y^{[i]}\rangle^{-5}\mathbf{1}_{2\delta\leq|x^{[i]}|\leq 4\delta}\sim e^{-(5l_{i}+\frac{5}{2})s}|z^{[i]}|^{-5}\mathbf{1}_{2\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}}\leq|z^{[i]}|\leq 4\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}}},

\displaystyle\left|\left(U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\left(\eta_{\delta}(x^{[i]})-\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})\right)\Theta_{l_{i}}(x^{[i]},t)\right|\lesssim\mu_{i}^{-1}\langle y^{[i]}\rangle^{-2}e^{-l_{i}s}\langle z^{[i]}\rangle^{2l_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{|z^{[i]}|\leq 2\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}},\ |y^{[i]}|>R}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\mu_{i}^{-1}\langle y^{[i]}\rangle^{-2}e^{-l_{i}s}\mathbf{1}_{|z^{[i]}|\leq 1,\ |y^{[i]}|>R}+e^{-(3l_{i}+1)s}|z^{[i]}|^{2l_{i}-2}\mathbf{1}_{1<|z^{[i]}|\leq 2\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}}}.

For
\psi\in\mathcal{X}_{\delta_{0},0}
,

\displaystyle\left|\left(U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\left(\eta_{2\delta}^{\frac{2}{3}}(x^{[i]})-\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})\right)\psi\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu_{i}^{-1}\langle y^{[i]}\rangle^{-2}\mathbf{1}_{|x^{[i]}|\leq 4\delta,\ |y^{[i]}|>R}\ \delta_{0}\Big[(T-t)^{l_{i}}\langle z^{[i]}\rangle^{2l_{i}+2}\mathbf{1}_{|z^{[i]}|\leq e^{\frac{l_{i}s}{2l_{i}+2}}}+\langle x\rangle^{-2}\mathbf{1}_{|z^{[i]}|>e^{\frac{l_{i}s}{2l_{i}+2}},\ |x^{[i]}|\leq 4\delta}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta_{0}\Big[\mu_{i}^{-1}\langle y^{[i]}\rangle^{-2}e^{-l_{i}s}\mathbf{1}_{|z^{[i]}|\leq 1,|y^{[i]}|>R}+e^{-(3l_{i}+1)s}|z^{[i]}|^{2l_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{1<|z^{[i]}|\leq e^{\frac{l_{i}s}{2l_{i}+2}}}+e^{-\big(2l_{i}+2-\frac{1}{l_{i}+1}\big)s}\langle x^{[i]}\rangle^{-2}\mathbf{1}_{e^{\frac{l_{i}s}{2l_{i}+2}}<|z^{[i]}|\leq 4\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}}}\Big],

where we used (
6.18
) and
\langle x\rangle\sim\langle x^{[i]}\rangle
for the last “
\lesssim
”.

For
|x^{[i]}|\leq 8\delta
,
u=U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\eta_{2\delta}(x^{[i]})+\Theta_{l_{i}}(x^{[i]},t)\eta_{\delta}(x^{[i]})+\mu_{i}^{-\frac{n-2}{2}}\phi_{i}(y^{[i]},t)\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})+\psi
.
By the elementary inequality

\left||a+b|^{p-1}(a+b)-|a|^{p-1}a-p|a|^{p-1}b\right|\leq p|b|^{p}

for
p\in(1,2]
,
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\eta_{4\delta}(x^{[i]})\mathcal{N}\left[\psi,\bm{\phi},\bm{\mu},\bm{\xi}\right]\right|=\eta_{4\delta}(x^{[i]})\Big||u|^{\frac{2}{3}}u-\left(U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right)^{\frac{5}{3}}\eta_{2\delta}^{\frac{5}{3}}(x^{[i]})

\displaystyle-\frac{5}{3}\left(U_{\mu_{i},\xi^{[i]}}(x)\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\eta_{2\delta}^{\frac{2}{3}}(x^{[i]})\left(\Theta_{l_{i}}(x^{[i]},t)\eta_{\delta}(x^{[i]})+\mu_{i}^{-\frac{3}{2}}\phi_{i}(y^{[i]},t)\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})+\psi(x,t)\right)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\eta_{4\delta}(x^{[i]})\left|\Theta_{l_{i}}(x^{[i]},t)\eta_{\delta}(x^{[i]})+\mu_{i}^{-\frac{3}{2}}\phi_{i}(y^{[i]},t)\eta_{R}(y^{[i]})+\psi(x,t)\right|^{\frac{5}{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle e^{-\frac{5}{3}l_{i}s}\langle z^{[i]}\rangle^{\frac{10}{3}l_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{|z^{[i]}|\leq 2\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}}}+R^{\frac{10}{3}}e^{-\frac{5}{3}l_{i}s}\langle y^{[i]}\rangle^{-\frac{25}{6}}\mathbf{1}_{|y^{[i]}|\leq 2R}

\displaystyle+\delta_{0}^{\frac{5}{3}}e^{-\frac{5}{3}l_{i}s}\langle z^{[i]}\rangle^{\frac{10}{3}l_{i}+\frac{10}{3}}\mathbf{1}_{|z^{[i]}|\leq e^{\frac{l_{i}s}{2l_{i}+2}}}+\delta_{0}^{\frac{5}{3}}\langle x^{[i]}\rangle^{-\frac{10}{3}}\mathbf{1}_{e^{\frac{l_{i}s}{2l_{i}+2}}<|z^{[i]}|\leq 8\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}}}.

Combining the estimates above, we have the estimate of
\mathcal{G}_{2,i}
.
∎

To exploit the spectrum properties of the
-A_{z}
, we give the following estimates.

Lemma 6.2
.

Under all assumptions in Lemma
6.1
, then for
i=1,2,\dots,\mathfrak{o}
,
s\in[s_{0},\infty)
, it holds that

\|g_{1,i}(\cdot,s)\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5})}\leq Ce^{-(2l_{i}+\frac{3}{4})s},\quad\|g_{2,i}(\cdot,s)\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\leq Ce^{-(\frac{5}{3}l_{i}+\frac{1}{2})s}

with a constant
C
independent of
T,\delta_{0}
and varying from line to line.
For
j\in\mathbb{N}
,

(6.19)

d_{i,j}(s)=e^{-\lambda_{j}s}\int_{s_{0*}}^{s}e^{\lambda_{j}\sigma}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{5}_{+}}g_{1,i}(z^{[i]},\sigma)e_{j}(z^{[i]})e^{-\frac{|z^{[i]}|^{2}}{4}}dz^{[i]}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}g_{2,i}(\tilde{z}^{[i]},\sigma)e_{j}(\tilde{z}^{[i]},0)e^{-\frac{|\tilde{z}^{[i]}|^{2}}{4}}d\tilde{z}^{[i]}\bigg]d\sigma

with
s_{0*}:=\begin{cases}s_{0}&\mbox{ \ if \ }\lambda_{j}\geq\frac{5}{3}l_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\\
\infty&\mbox{ \ if \ }\lambda_{j}<\frac{5}{3}l_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\end{cases}

are integrable and satisfy (
6.14
). Moreover, given
k\in\mathbb{N}
, for
j=0,1,\dots,k
,

(6.20)

\displaystyle|d_{i,j}(s)|+|\dot{d}_{i,j}(s)|\leq C\begin{cases}e^{-(\frac{5}{3}l_{i}+\frac{1}{2})s}&\mbox{ \ if \ }\lambda_{j}\neq\frac{5}{3}l_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\\
se^{-(\frac{5}{3}l_{i}+\frac{1}{2})s}&\mbox{ \ if \ }\lambda_{j}=\frac{5}{3}l_{i}+\frac{1}{2},\end{cases}

(6.21)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k}\big(|\dot{d}_{i,j}(s)|+|d_{i,j}(s)|\big)\left(\left|A_{z^{[i]}}\tilde{e}_{j}(z^{[i]})\right|+\left|\tilde{e}_{j}(z^{[i]})\right|\right)\leq C\mathbf{1}_{|z^{[i]}|\leq 2C_{\tilde{e}}}\begin{cases}e^{-(\frac{5}{3}l_{i}+\frac{1}{2})s}&\mbox{ \ if \ }\lambda_{k}<\frac{5}{3}l_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\\
se^{-(\frac{5}{3}l_{i}+\frac{1}{2})s}&\mbox{ \ if \ }\lambda_{k}\geq\frac{5}{3}l_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\end{cases}

with
\tilde{e}_{j}
given in (
6.1
).
In particular,

(6.22)

\|\tilde{g}_{1,i}(\cdot,s)\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5})}\leq Cse^{-(\frac{5}{3}l_{i}+\frac{1}{2})s}.

Proof.

By (
6.9
), Lemme
6.1
, (
6.18
),

\displaystyle\|g_{1,i}(\cdot,s)\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5})}\lesssim e^{-s}R^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mu_{i}^{-2}e^{-l_{i}s}\big\|\langle\mu_{i}^{-1}\left(T-t\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}z^{[i]}\rangle^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{1}_{|z^{[i]}|\leq 4R\mu_{i}(T-t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle+\langle\mu_{i}^{-1}\left(T-t\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}z^{[i]}\rangle^{-\frac{11}{4}}\mathbf{1}_{2^{-1}R\mu_{i}(T-t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}<|z^{[i]}|\leq 1}\big\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5})}

\displaystyle+e^{-s}\big\|e^{-(3l_{i}+\frac{1}{2})s}|z^{[i]}|^{-3}\mathbf{1}_{1<|z^{[i]}|\leq 4\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}}}+\mathbf{1}_{\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}}\leq|z^{[i]}|\leq 2\delta e^{\frac{s}{2}}}\big\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle e^{-(2l_{i}+\frac{3}{4})s}+e^{-s}\left[e^{-(3l_{i}+\frac{1}{2})s}+e^{-\frac{1}{16}\delta^{2}e^{s}}\right]\sim e^{-(2l_{i}+\frac{3}{4})s},

where we used

\displaystyle\big\|\langle\mu_{i}^{-1}\left(T-t\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}z^{[i]}\rangle^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{1}_{|z^{[i]}|\leq 4R\mu_{i}(T-t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\big\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5})}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbf{1}_{|z^{[i]}|\leq\mu_{i}(T-t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}}+\mu_{i}^{\frac{9}{4}}\left(T-t\right)^{-\frac{9}{8}}|z^{[i]}|^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{1}_{\mu_{i}(T-t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}<|z^{[i]}|\leq 4R\mu_{i}(T-t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\big\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu_{i}^{\frac{5}{2}}(T-t)^{-\frac{5}{4}}+R^{\frac{1}{4}}\mu_{i}^{\frac{5}{2}}(T-t)^{-\frac{5}{4}}\sim R^{\frac{1}{4}}e^{-(5l_{i}+\frac{15}{4})s},

\displaystyle\big\|\langle\mu_{i}^{-1}\left(T-t\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}z^{[i]}\rangle^{-\frac{11}{4}}\mathbf{1}_{2^{-1}R\mu_{i}(T-t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}<|z^{[i]}|\leq 1}\big\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5})}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\mu_{i}^{\frac{11}{4}}(T-t)^{-\frac{11}{8}}\big\||z^{[i]}|^{-\frac{11}{4}}\mathbf{1}_{2^{-1}R\mu_{i}(T-t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}<|z^{[i]}|\leq 1}\big\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5})}\sim R^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mu_{i}^{\frac{5}{2}}(T-t)^{-\frac{5}{4}}\sim R^{-\frac{1}{4}}e^{-(5l_{i}+\frac{15}{4})s}.

\|g_{2,i}(\cdot,s)\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\lesssim e^{-\frac{s}{2}}\left[R^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mu_{i}^{-1}e^{-l_{i}s}e^{-(\frac{7}{2}l_{i}+\frac{21}{8})s}+e^{-\frac{5}{3}l_{i}s}+\left(e^{-\frac{5}{3}l_{i}s}+\delta_{0}^{\frac{5}{3}}\right)e^{-\frac{1}{16}e^{\frac{l_{i}}{l_{i}+1}s}}\right]\sim e^{-(\frac{5}{3}l_{i}+\frac{1}{2})s},

where we used (
6.18
) and

\displaystyle\|\langle\tilde{y}^{[i]}\rangle^{-\frac{7}{4}}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{z}^{[i]}|\leq 1}\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}\sim\|\langle\mu_{i}^{-1}\left(T-t\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{z}^{[i]}\rangle^{-\frac{7}{4}}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{z}^{[i]}|\leq 1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{z}^{[i]}|\leq\mu_{i}\left(T-t\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}}+\mu_{i}^{\frac{7}{4}}\left(T-t\right)^{-\frac{7}{8}}|\tilde{z}^{[i]}|^{-\frac{7}{4}}\mathbf{1}_{\mu_{i}\left(T-t\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}<|\tilde{z}^{[i]}|\leq 1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mu_{i}^{2}(T-t)^{-1}+\mu_{i}^{\frac{7}{4}}\left(T-t\right)^{-\frac{7}{8}}\sim e^{-(\frac{7}{2}l_{i}+\frac{21}{8})s}.

It follows that

\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{5}_{+}}g_{1,i}(z^{[i]},s)e_{j}(z^{[i]})e^{-\frac{|z^{[i]}|^{2}}{4}}dz^{[i]}\bigg|+\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}g_{2,i}(\tilde{z}^{[i]},s)e_{j}(\tilde{z}^{[i]},0)e^{-\frac{|\tilde{z}^{[i]}|^{2}}{4}}d\tilde{z}^{[i]}\bigg|\lesssim e^{-(\frac{5}{3}l_{i}+\frac{1}{2})s}.

Now it is easy to get that
d_{i,j}(s)
given in (
6.19
) are well-defined and satisfy (
6.20
).
Due to the support of
\tilde{e}_{j}
, we deduce (
6.21
).
The bound of
\|\tilde{g}_{1,i}(\cdot,s)\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5})}
is deduced by (
6.12
), the estimate of
\|g_{1,i}(\cdot,s)\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5})}
, and
(
6.21
).
∎

6.2.
Estimates of
\Phi_{i}
satisfying (
6.11
),
i=1,2,\dots,\mathfrak{o}

This subsection is inspired by
[
25
,
60
]
. We will derive the estimate of
\|\Phi_{i}(\cdot,s)\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{5}_{+})}
and then give the pointwise estimate of
\Phi_{i}
.

Lemma 6.3
.

Under all assumptions in Lemma
6.1
, then there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
T,\delta_{0}
such that

\|\Phi_{i}(\cdot,s)\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{5}_{+})}\leq Cse^{-(\frac{5}{3}l_{i}+\frac{1}{2})s}.

Proof.

We first assume
\mathcal{G}_{1,i},\mathcal{G}_{2,i}
are smooth about
z^{[i]},s
and
\tilde{z}^{[i]},s
respectively and the derivatives of
\mathcal{G}_{1,i},\mathcal{G}_{2,i}
belong to
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5}\times(s_{0},s))
and
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{4}\times(s_{0},s))
respectively for all
s\in(s_{0},\infty)
. Then
\Phi_{i}
is smooth and the derivatives of
\Phi_{i}
are bounded for any fixed
s\in(s_{0},\infty)
.
For
j=0,1,\dots,N(\lceil 5l_{i}/3\rceil+1)
, testing the equation (
6.11
) by
e_{j}(z^{[i]})\rho(z^{[i]})
, integrating by parts as (
2.6
), and using (
2.11
), (
6.13
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\partial_{s}(\Phi_{i}(\cdot,s),e_{j})_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5})}=(\Phi_{i}(\cdot,s),A_{z^{[i]}}e_{j})_{{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5})}}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{5}_{+}}\tilde{g}_{1,i}(z^{[i]},s)e_{j}(z^{[i]})e^{-\frac{|z^{[i]}|^{2}}{4}}dz^{[i]}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}g_{2,i}(\tilde{z}^{[i]},s)e_{j}(\tilde{z}^{[i]},0)e^{-\frac{|\tilde{z}^{[i]}|^{2}}{4}}d\tilde{z}^{[i]}=-\lambda_{j}(\Phi_{i}(\cdot,s),e_{j})_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5})}.

By
\Phi_{i}(\cdot,s_{0})=0
, we have

(6.23)

(\Phi_{i}(\cdot,s),e_{j})_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5})}=0,\quad j=0,1,\dots,N(\lceil 5l_{i}/3\rceil+1)\quad\mbox{ \ for all \ }s\geq s_{0}.

Testing the equation (
6.11
) by
\Phi_{i}\rho(z^{[i]})
and integrating by parts as (
2.6
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{s}\left(\|\Phi_{i}(\cdot,s)\|^{2}_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{5}_{+})}\right)=-\|\nabla\Phi_{i}(\cdot,s)\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{5}_{+})}^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{5}_{+}}\tilde{g}_{1,i}(z^{[i]},s)\Phi_{i}(z^{[i]},s)e^{-\frac{|z^{[i]}|^{2}}{4}}dz^{[i]}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}g_{2,i}(\tilde{z}^{[i]},s)\Phi_{i}((\tilde{z}^{[i]},0),s)e^{-\frac{|\tilde{z}^{[i]}|^{2}}{4}}d\tilde{z}^{[i]}\leq-\|\nabla\Phi_{i}(\cdot,s)\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{5}_{+})}^{2}+Cf_{1}(s)\|\Phi_{i}(\cdot,s)\|_{H_{\rho}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{5}_{+})}

with
f_{1}(s):=\|g_{1,i}(\cdot,s)\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5})}+\|g_{2,i}(\cdot,s)\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{4})}+\sum_{j=0}^{N(\lceil 5l_{i}/3\rceil+1)}\left(|d_{i,j}(s)|+|\dot{d}_{i,j}(s)|\right)
,
where we used (
6.12
) and Lemma
C.3
for the last inequality.
Then by (
6.23
), Lemma
2.3
(
3
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{s}\left(\|\Phi_{i}(\cdot,s)\|^{2}_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{5}_{+})}\right)\leq-\left(1-\epsilon\right)\left(\lceil 5l_{i}/3\rceil+3/2\right)\|\Phi_{i}(\cdot,s)\|_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{5}_{+})}^{2}+\frac{C(\epsilon)}{2}(f_{1}(s))^{2},

where
\epsilon>0
can be close to
0
arbitrarily. By
\Phi_{i}(\cdot,s_{0})=0
,

(6.24)

\|\Phi_{i}(\cdot,s)\|^{2}_{L_{\rho}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{5}_{+})}\leq e^{-(1-\epsilon)\left(2\lceil 5l_{i}/3\rceil+3\right)s}\int_{s_{0}}^{s}C(\epsilon)(f_{1}(a))^{2}e^{(1-\epsilon)\left(2\lceil 5l_{i}/3\rceil+3\right)a}da.

Generally, when
\mathcal{G}_{1,i},\mathcal{G}_{2,i}
are not smooth, we apply the mollifier to
\mathcal{G}_{1,i},\mathcal{G}_{2,i}
in spatial and time variables. Then repeating the above process and taking the limitation will deduce (
6.24
).
Using Lemma
6.2
and
taking
\epsilon
sufficiently small to make
\left(1-\epsilon\right)\left(2\lceil 5l_{i}/3\rceil+3\right)>10l_{i}/3+1
, we get the desired estimate.
∎

The representation formula of
\Phi_{i}
in (
6.11
) is given by (
2.21
) of the form

\Phi_{i}(z^{[i]},s)=\int_{s_{0}}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{5}}H_{5}\left(z^{[i]},s,w,\sigma\right)\tilde{g}_{1,i}(w,\sigma)dwd\sigma+\int_{s_{0}}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}H_{5}\left(z^{[i]},s,(\tilde{w},0),\sigma\right)g_{2,i}(\tilde{w},\sigma)d\tilde{w}d\sigma.

Lemma 6.4
.

Under all assumptions in Lemma
6.1
, then there exists a constant
C>0
