g^{\prime}(r)=r^{a-1}\left[a\left(1+\ln r\right)+1\right]
and
g^{\prime}(r)<0

for
r\geq C_{1}
with a large constant
C_{1}=C_{1}(a)>1
.
For
r\in\left[r_{0},C_{1}\right]
,
g(r)\sim C(C_{1},r_{0})
. Thus, (
B.5
) holds.

(
B.6
) is deduced by changing the variable
z=r_{0}^{-1}r
and the above result.

(
5
). For any
M>\max\left\{r_{0},1\right\}
,
r\in\left[r_{0},M\right]
, we have

\int_{r}^{\infty}x^{a}\left(1+\left|\ln x\right|\right)^{b}dx\leq C(a,b,r_{0},M)r^{a+1}\left(1+\left|\ln r\right|\right)^{b}.

For
r>M
,

\int_{r}^{\infty}x^{a}\left(1+\ln x\right)^{b}dx=\frac{-1}{a+1}r^{a+1}\left(1+\ln r\right)^{b}-\frac{b}{a+1}\int_{r}^{\infty}x^{a}\left(1+\ln x\right)^{b-1}dx,

where

\left|\frac{b}{a+1}\int_{r}^{\infty}x^{a}\left(1+\ln x\right)^{b-1}dx\right|\leq\frac{1}{9}\int_{r}^{\infty}x^{a}\left(1+\ln x\right)^{b}dx

for
M=M(a,b)
sufficiently large. Thus, we conclude (
B.7
).

∎

B.2.
Beyond Neumann boundary value
v(t)|\tilde{x}|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{l_{1}(t)\leq|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{2}(t)}
and
v(t)\left(|\tilde{x}|+l_{1}(t)\right)^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{2}(t)}

Lemma B.2
.

Let
n>2
be an integer,
t>t_{0}\geq 0
,
b\in\mathbb{R}
. Suppose that

v(s)\geq 0
for
s\in[t_{0},t]
;
0\leq l_{1}(s)\leq l_{2}(s)\leq C_{*}s^{\frac{1}{2}}
for
s\in[t_{0},t]
,

C_{l}^{-1}l_{1}(t)\leq l_{1}(s)
, and
l_{2}(s)\leq C_{l}l_{2}(t)
, for all
s\in[\max\{t_{0},\frac{t}{2}\},t]
, where
C_{*}>0,C_{l}\geq 1
are constants. Given
x=(\tilde{x},x_{n})
,
\tilde{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
,
x_{n}\geq 0
,
C_{0}>0
, we define

\mathcal{T}\left[f\right](x,t):=\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{|\tilde{x}-y|^{2}+x_{n}^{2}}{t-s}}f(y,s)dyds

for an admissible function
f
.
Then for any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, we have

\mathcal{T}\left[v(t)|\tilde{x}|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{l_{1}(t)\leq|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{2}(t)}\right](x,t)\leq C\Big(w_{1}+\sup\limits_{t_{1}\in\left[\max\{t_{0},t/2\},t\right]}v(t_{1})w_{2}\Big),

where
C
is a constant only depending on
n,b,C_{*},C_{l},C_{0}
,

\displaystyle w_{1}:=

\displaystyle t^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t-t_{0}}}\left(\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{x}|\leq C_{*}\left[1+\left(1-\epsilon\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\epsilon^{-1}t^{\frac{1}{2}}}+e^{-C_{0}\left(1-\epsilon\right)\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{t-t_{0}}}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{x}|>C_{*}\left[1+\left(1-\epsilon\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\epsilon^{-1}t^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\int_{t_{0}}^{\max\{t_{0},\frac{t}{2}\}}v(s)\begin{cases}l_{2}^{n-1-b}(s),&b<n-1\\
\ln(\frac{l_{2}(s)}{l_{1}(s)}),&b=n-1\\
l_{1}^{n-1-b}(s),&b>n-1\end{cases}ds,

w_{2}:=\begin{cases}\begin{cases}l_{2}^{1-b}(t),&b<1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle,&b=1\\
l_{1}^{1-b}(t),&b>1\end{cases},&|x|\leq l_{1}(t)\\
\begin{cases}l_{2}^{1-b}(t),&b<1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{|x|})\rangle,&b=1\\
|x|^{1-b},&1<b<n-1\\
|x|^{2-n}\langle\ln(\frac{|x|}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle,&b=n-1\\
|x|^{2-n}l_{1}^{n-1-b}(t),&b>n-1\end{cases},&l_{1}(t)<|x|\leq l_{2}(t)\\
\begin{cases}l_{2}^{n-1-b}(t),&b<n-1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle,&b=n-1\\
l_{1}^{n-1-b}(t),&b>n-1\end{cases}\begin{cases}|x|^{2-n},&l_{2}(t)<|x|\leq C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
|x|^{-2}t^{2-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-2C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}+\frac{64}{81}|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{t}},&|x|>C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}\end{cases}\end{cases}

with the convention
\frac{l_{2}(s)}{l_{1}(s)}=1

\left(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)}=1\right)
if
l_{1}(s)=l_{2}(s)=0

\left(l_{1}(t)=l_{2}(t)=0\right)
.

Under the additional assumption that
l_{1}(s)\leq C_{l}l_{1}(t)
for all
s\in[\max\{t_{0},\frac{t}{2}\},t]
, one replacing
\ln(\frac{l_{2}(s)}{l_{1}(s)})
by
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(s)}{l_{1}(s)})\rangle
in the definition of
w_{1}
, then the same upper bound is held for

\mathcal{T}\left[v(t)\left(|\tilde{x}|+l_{1}(t)\right)^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{2}(t)}\right]
.

Proof.

In this proof, we assume
\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\cdots ds=0
if
t_{1}\geq t_{2}
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\mathbf{1}_{c_{1}\leq|y|\leq c_{2}}\cdots dy=0
if
c_{1}\geq c_{2}
.
We emphasize that all “
\lesssim
”, “
\sim
” in this proof are independent of
\epsilon
,
t_{0}
. Denote
t_{*}:=\max\{t_{0},\frac{t}{2}\}
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\left[v(t)|\tilde{x}|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{l_{1}(t)\leq|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{2}(t)}\right]\lesssim t^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t-t_{0}}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{*}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{|\tilde{x}-y|^{2}}{t-t_{0}}}v(s)|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{l_{1}(s)\leq|y|\leq l_{2}(s)}dyds

\displaystyle+\sup\limits_{t_{1}\in[t_{*},t]}v(t_{1})\int_{t_{*}}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t-s}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{|\tilde{x}-y|^{2}}{t-s}}|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{l_{1}(s)\leq|y|\leq l_{2}(s)}dyds:=u_{1}+\sup\limits_{t_{1}\in[t_{*},t]}v(t_{1})\tilde{u}_{2}.

Obviously,

\tilde{u}_{2}\leq u_{2}:=\int_{t_{*}}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t-s}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{|\tilde{x}-y|^{2}}{t-s}}|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{C_{l}^{-1}l_{1}(t)\leq|y|\leq C_{l}l_{2}(t)}dyds.

For
u_{1}
, notice
|y|\leq C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}
. For
|\tilde{x}|\leq Mt^{\frac{1}{2}}
, we use
e^{-C_{0}\frac{|\tilde{x}-y|^{2}}{t-t_{0}}}\leq 1
;
Given a constant
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, for
|\tilde{x}|>Mt^{\frac{1}{2}}
with
M=C_{*}\left[1+\left(1-\epsilon\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\epsilon^{-1}
, one has
|\tilde{x}-y|\geq\left(1-M^{-1}C_{*}\right)|\tilde{x}|\geq\left(1-\epsilon\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}|\tilde{x}|
. Then,
u_{1}\lesssim w_{1}
.

Let us estimate
u_{2}
in different regions.

For
|\tilde{x}|\leq 2^{-1}C_{l}^{-1}l_{1}(t)
,
we have
|\tilde{x}-y|^{2}\geq 4^{-1}|y|^{2}
, and then

\displaystyle u_{2}\leq\int_{t_{*}}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t-s}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}e^{-4^{-1}C_{0}\frac{|y|^{2}}{t-s}}|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{C_{l}^{-1}l_{1}(t)\leq|y|\leq C_{l}l_{2}(t)}dyds

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\left(\int_{t_{*}}^{t-\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{(9C_{*})^{2}}}+\int_{t-\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{(9C_{*})^{2}}}^{t-\frac{l_{1}^{2}(t)}{(9C_{*})^{2}}}+\int_{t-\frac{l_{1}^{2}(t)}{(9C_{*})^{2}}}^{t}\right)e^{-C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t-s}}(t-s)^{-\frac{b+1}{2}}\int_{4^{-1}C_{0}C_{l}^{-2}\frac{l_{1}^{2}(t)}{t-s}}^{4^{-1}C_{0}C_{l}^{2}\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{t-s}}e^{-z}z^{\frac{n-b}{2}-\frac{3}{2}}dzds:=u_{21}+u_{22}+u_{23}.

For
u_{21}
, using
e^{-z}\sim 1
and
t_{*}\geq\frac{t}{2}
, we have

u_{21}\lesssim x_{n}^{2-n}\int_{2C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t}}^{C_{0}\frac{{(9C_{*})^{2}}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{2}^{2}(t)}}e^{-r}r^{\frac{n}{2}-2}dr\begin{cases}l_{2}^{n-b-1}(t),&b<n-1\\
\ln\left(C_{l}^{2}\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)}\right),&b=n-1\\
l_{1}^{n-b-1}(t),&b>n-1,\end{cases}

where we always use the convention
\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)}=1
if
l_{1}(t)=l_{2}(t)=0
.
Since
n>2
, we have
\frac{n}{2}-2>-1
. Then

u_{21}\lesssim\begin{cases}l_{2}^{n-b-1}(t),&b<n-1\\
\ln\left(C_{l}^{2}\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)}\right),&b=n-1\\
l_{1}^{n-b-1}(t),&b>n-1\end{cases}\begin{cases}l_{2}^{2-n}(t),&x_{n}\leq l_{2}(t)\\
x_{n}^{2-n},&l_{2}(t)<x_{n}\leq C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
x_{n}^{-2}t^{2-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-2C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t}},&x_{n}>C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}},\end{cases}

where we used (
B.1
) for the case
x_{n}>C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

For
u_{22}
, since
\frac{l_{1}^{2}(t)}{(9C_{*})^{2}}\leq t-s\leq\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{(9C_{*})^{2}}
, then

\displaystyle u_{22}\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{t-\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{(9C_{*})^{2}}}^{t-\frac{l_{1}^{2}(t)}{(9C_{*})^{2}}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t-s}}(t-s)^{-\frac{b+1}{2}}\begin{cases}1,&b<n-1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{t-s}{l_{1}^{2}(t)})\rangle,&b=n-1\\
(\frac{l_{1}^{2}(t)}{t-s})^{\frac{n-b}{2}-\frac{1}{2}},&b>n-1\end{cases}ds

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\begin{cases}x_{n}^{1-b}\int_{C_{0}\frac{(9C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{2}^{2}(t)}}^{C_{0}\frac{(9C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{1}^{2}(t)}}e^{-r}r^{\frac{b}{2}-\frac{3}{2}}dr,&b<n-1\\
x_{n}^{2-n}\int_{C_{0}\frac{(9C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{2}^{2}(t)}}^{C_{0}\frac{(9C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{1}^{2}(t)}}e^{-r}r^{\frac{n}{2}-2}\langle\ln(\frac{C_{0}x_{n}^{2}}{rl_{1}^{2}(t)})\rangle dr,&b=n-1\\
l_{1}^{n-b-1}(t)x_{n}^{2-n}\int_{C_{0}\frac{(9C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{2}^{2}(t)}}^{C_{0}\frac{(9C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{1}^{2}(t)}}e^{-r}r^{\frac{n}{2}-2}dr,&b>n-1.\end{cases}

Since
n>2
, we have

u_{22}\lesssim\begin{cases}\begin{cases}l_{2}^{1-b}(t),&b<1\\
\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)}),&b=1\\
l_{1}^{1-b}(t),&b>1\end{cases},&x_{n}\leq l_{1}(t)\\
\begin{cases}l_{2}^{1-b}(t),&b<1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{x_{n}})\rangle,&b=1\\
x_{n}^{1-b},&1<b<n-1\\
x_{n}^{2-n}\langle\ln(\frac{x_{n}}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle,&b=n-1\\
x_{n}^{2-n}l_{1}^{n-b-1}(t),&b>n-1\end{cases},&l_{1}(t)<x_{n}\leq l_{2}(t)\\
\begin{cases}x_{n}^{-2}l_{2}^{3-b}(t)e^{-C_{0}\frac{(9C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{2}^{2}(t)}},&b<n-1\\
x_{n}^{-2}l_{2}^{4-n}(t)\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle e^{-C_{0}\frac{(9C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{2}^{2}(t)}},&b=n-1\\
x_{n}^{-2}l_{2}^{4-n}(t)l_{1}^{n-b-1}(t)e^{-C_{0}\frac{(9C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{2}^{2}(t)}},&b>n-1\end{cases},&x_{n}>l_{2}(t),\end{cases}

where we used (
B.2
), (
B.7
) for the case
l_{1}(t)<x_{n}\leq l_{2}(t)
,
b=n-1
,
and (
B.1
), (
B.2
) for the case
x_{n}>l_{2}(t)
.

For
u_{23}
, by (
B.1
), we have

\displaystyle u_{23}\lesssim l_{1}^{n-b-3}(t)\int_{t-\frac{l_{1}^{2}(t)}{(9C_{*})^{2}}}^{t}e^{-C_{0}\left(x_{n}^{2}+4^{-1}C_{l}^{-2}l_{1}^{2}(t)\right)\frac{1}{t-s}}(t-s)^{1-\frac{n}{2}}ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle l_{1}^{n-b-3}(t)\left[C_{0}\left(x_{n}^{2}+4^{-1}C_{l}^{-2}l_{1}^{2}(t)\right)\right]^{2-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{C_{0}(9C_{*})^{2}\frac{x_{n}^{2}+4^{-1}C_{l}^{-2}l_{1}^{2}(t)}{l_{1}^{2}(t)}}^{\infty}e^{-r}r^{\frac{n}{2}-3}dr

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle l_{1}^{3-b}(t)\left(x_{n}^{2}+4^{-1}C_{l}^{-2}l_{1}^{2}(t)\right)^{-1}e^{-C_{0}(9C_{*})^{2}\frac{x_{n}^{2}+4^{-1}C_{l}^{-2}l_{1}^{2}(t)}{l_{1}^{2}(t)}}\sim l_{1}^{3-b}(t)\left(x_{n}^{2}+l_{1}^{2}(t)\right)^{-1}e^{-C_{0}\frac{(9C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{1}^{2}(t)}}.

In sum, when
n>2
, for
|\tilde{x}|\leq 2^{-1}C_{l}^{-1}l_{1}(t)
, we have

u_{2}\lesssim w_{21}:=\begin{cases}\begin{cases}l_{2}^{1-b}(t),&b<1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle,&b=1\\
l_{1}^{1-b}(t),&b>1\end{cases},&x_{n}\leq l_{1}(t)\\
\begin{cases}l_{2}^{1-b}(t),&b<1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{x_{n}})\rangle,&b=1\\
x_{n}^{1-b},&1<b<n-1\\
x_{n}^{2-n}\langle\ln(\frac{x_{n}}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle,&b=n-1\\
x_{n}^{2-n}l_{1}^{n-1-b}(t),&b>n-1\end{cases},&l_{1}(t)<x_{n}\leq l_{2}(t)\\
\begin{cases}l_{2}^{n-1-b}(t),&b<n-1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle,&b=n-1\\
l_{1}^{n-1-b}(t),&b>n-1\end{cases}\begin{cases}x_{n}^{2-n},&l_{2}(t)<x_{n}\leq C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
x_{n}^{-2}t^{2-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-2C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t}},&x_{n}>C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}},\end{cases}\end{cases}

where we used (
B.6
) for the case
l_{1}(t)<x_{n}\leq l_{2}(t)
,
b=n-1
, and Lemma
B.1
(
3
) for the case
x_{n}>C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

For
2^{-1}C_{l}^{-1}l_{1}(t)<|\tilde{x}|\leq\min\left\{9C_{l}l_{2}(t),9C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}
,

\displaystyle u_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t-s}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{|\tilde{x}-y|^{2}}{t-s}}|y|^{-b}\left(\mathbf{1}_{C_{l}^{-1}l_{1}(t)\leq|y|<\frac{|\tilde{x}|}{2}}+\mathbf{1}_{\frac{|\tilde{x}|}{2}\leq|y|\leq 2|\tilde{x}|}+\mathbf{1}_{2|\tilde{x}|<|y|\leq C_{l}l_{2}(t)}\right)dyds

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle u_{21}+u_{22}+u_{23}.

For
u_{21}
, since
|\tilde{x}-y|\geq\frac{|\tilde{x}|}{2}
, we have

\displaystyle u_{21}\lesssim\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\left(x_{n}^{2}+\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{4}\right)\frac{1}{t-s}}ds\begin{cases}|\tilde{x}|^{n-1-b},&b<n-1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{|\tilde{x}|}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle,&b=n-1\\
l_{1}^{n-1-b}(t),&b>n-1\end{cases}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[C_{0}\left(x_{n}^{2}+\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{4}\right)\right]^{1-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{2C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}+\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{4}}{t}}^{\infty}e^{-r}r^{\frac{n}{2}-2}dr\begin{cases}|\tilde{x}|^{n-1-b},&b<n-1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{|\tilde{x}|}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle,&b=n-1\\
l_{1}^{n-1-b}(t),&b>n-1.\end{cases}

Since
n>2
, using (
B.1
) for the case
x_{n}>t^{\frac{1}{2}}
,
we have

u_{21}\lesssim\begin{cases}|\tilde{x}|^{n-1-b},&b<n-1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{|\tilde{x}|}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle,&b=n-1\\
l_{1}^{n-1-b}(t),&b>n-1\end{cases}\begin{cases}|x|^{2-n},&x_{n}\leq t^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
|x|^{-2}t^{2-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-2C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t}},&x_{n}>t^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{cases}

For
u_{22}
, using
n>1
, we have

\displaystyle u_{22}\lesssim|\tilde{x}|^{-b}\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t-s}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{|\tilde{x}-y|^{2}}{t-s}}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{x}-y|\leq 3|\tilde{x}|}dyds

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle|\tilde{x}|^{-b}\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t-s}}\int_{0}^{9C_{0}\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{t-s}}e^{-z}z^{\frac{n}{2}-\frac{3}{2}}dzds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|\tilde{x}|^{-b}\left[|\tilde{x}|^{n-1}\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t-\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{(99C_{*})^{2}}}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t-s}}ds+\int_{t-\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{(99C_{*})^{2}}}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t-s}}ds\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\tilde{x}|^{-b}\left[|\tilde{x}|^{n-1}\left(C_{0}x_{n}^{2}\right)^{1-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{2C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t}}^{C_{0}\frac{(99C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}}e^{-r}r^{\frac{n}{2}-2}dr+\left(C_{0}x_{n}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{C_{0}\frac{(99C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}}^{\infty}e^{-r}r^{-\frac{3}{2}}dr\right].

Since
n>2
, using (
B.1
) to the case
|\tilde{x}|<x_{n}\leq t^{\frac{1}{2}}
, and (
B.1
), (
B.3
) to the case
x_{n}>t^{\frac{1}{2}}
, we have

u_{22}\lesssim\begin{cases}|\tilde{x}|^{1-b},&x_{n}\leq|\tilde{x}|\\
x_{n}^{2-n}|\tilde{x}|^{n-1-b},&|\tilde{x}|<x_{n}\leq t^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
x_{n}^{-2}|\tilde{x}|^{n-1-b}t^{2-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-2C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t}},&x_{n}>t^{\frac{1}{2}},\end{cases}

where we assume that if
|\tilde{x}|\geq t^{\frac{1}{2}}
, the case
|\tilde{x}|<x_{n}\leq t^{\frac{1}{2}}
is vacuum, and the cases
x_{n}\leq|\tilde{x}|
and
x_{n}>t^{\frac{1}{2}}
have the common part
t^{\frac{1}{2}}<x_{n}\leq|\tilde{x}|
with the same upper bound up to a multiplicity of a constant. The same convention is used for the other similar conditions.

For
u_{23}
,

\displaystyle u_{23}\leq\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t-s}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}e^{-\frac{C_{0}}{4}\frac{|y|^{2}}{t-s}}|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{2|\tilde{x}|<|y|\leq C_{l}l_{2}(t)}dyds

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t-\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{(9C_{*})^{2}}}+\int_{t-\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{(9C_{*})^{2}}}^{t-\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{(99C_{l}C_{*})^{2}}}+\int_{t-\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{{(99C_{l}C_{*})^{2}}}}^{t}\right)(t-s)^{-\frac{b}{2}-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t-s}}\int_{C_{0}\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{t-s}}^{\frac{C_{0}C_{l}^{2}}{4}\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{t-s}}e^{-z}z^{\frac{n-b}{2}-\frac{3}{2}}dzds:=u_{231}+u_{232}+u_{233},

where
\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{(99C_{l}C_{*})^{2}}\leq\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{(9C_{*})^{2}}\leq\frac{t}{2}
.

For
u_{231}
, using
e^{-z}\sim 1
for this part, we have

\displaystyle u_{231}\lesssim\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t-\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{(9C_{*})^{2}}}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t-s}}ds\begin{cases}l_{2}^{n-1-b}(t),&b<n-1\\
\langle\ln\left(|\tilde{x}|^{-1}l_{2}(t)\right)\rangle,&b=n-1\\
|\tilde{x}|^{n-1-b},&b>n-1\end{cases}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(C_{0}x_{n}^{2}\right)^{1-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{2C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t}}^{C_{0}\frac{(9C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{2}^{2}(t)}}e^{-r}r^{\frac{n}{2}-2}dr\begin{cases}l_{2}^{n-1-b}(t),&b<n-1\\
\langle\ln\left(|\tilde{x}|^{-1}l_{2}(t)\right)\rangle,&b=n-1\\
|\tilde{x}|^{n-1-b},&b>n-1.\end{cases}

Since
n>2
, using (
B.1
) for the case
x_{n}>C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}
, we have

u_{231}\lesssim\begin{cases}l_{2}^{n-1-b}(t),&b<n-1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{|\tilde{x}|})\rangle,&b=n-1\\
|\tilde{x}|^{n-1-b},&b>n-1\end{cases}\begin{cases}l_{2}^{2-n}(t),&x_{n}\leq l_{2}(t)\\
x_{n}^{2-n},&l_{2}(t)<x_{n}\leq C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
x_{n}^{-2}t^{2-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-2C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t}},&x_{n}>C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{cases}

For
u_{232}
, since
\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{(99C_{l}C_{*})^{2}}\leq t-s\leq\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{(9C_{*})^{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle u_{232}\lesssim\begin{cases}\int_{t-\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{(9C_{*})^{2}}}^{t-\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{(99C_{l}C_{*})^{2}}}(t-s)^{-\frac{b}{2}-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t-s}}ds,&b<n-1\\
\int_{t-\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{(9C_{*})^{2}}}^{t-\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{(99C_{l}C_{*})^{2}}}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t-s}}\langle\ln\left(|\tilde{x}|^{-2}(t-s)\right)\rangle ds,&b=n-1\\
|\tilde{x}|^{n-1-b}\int_{t-\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{(9C_{*})^{2}}}^{t-\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{(99C_{l}C_{*})^{2}}}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t-s}}ds,&b>n-1\end{cases}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\left(C_{0}x_{n}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1-b}{2}}\int_{C_{0}\frac{(9C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{2}^{2}(t)}}^{C_{0}\frac{(99C_{l}C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}}e^{-r}r^{\frac{b}{2}-\frac{3}{2}}dr,&b<n-1\\
(C_{0}x_{n}^{2})^{1-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{C_{0}\frac{(9C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{2}^{2}(t)}}^{C_{0}\frac{(99C_{l}C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}}e^{-r}r^{\frac{n}{2}-2}\langle\ln(\frac{C_{0}x_{n}^{2}}{r|\tilde{x}|^{2}})\rangle dr,&b=n-1\\
|\tilde{x}|^{n-1-b}(C_{0}x_{n}^{2})^{1-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{C_{0}\frac{(9C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{2}^{2}(t)}}^{C_{0}\frac{(99C_{l}C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}}e^{-r}r^{\frac{n}{2}-2}dr,&b>n-1.\end{cases}

Since
n>2
, we have

u_{232}\lesssim\begin{cases}\begin{cases}l_{2}^{1-b}(t),&b<1\\
\left|\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{|\tilde{x}|})\right|,&b=1\\
|\tilde{x}|^{1-b},&b>1\end{cases},&x_{n}\leq|\tilde{x}|\\
\begin{cases}l_{2}^{1-b}(t),&b<1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{x_{n}})\rangle,&b=1\\
x_{n}^{1-b},&1<b<n-1\\
x_{n}^{2-n}\langle\ln(\frac{x_{n}}{|\tilde{x}|})\rangle,&b=n-1\\
x_{n}^{2-n}|\tilde{x}|^{n-1-b},&b>n-1\end{cases},&|\tilde{x}|<x_{n}\leq l_{2}(t)\\
\begin{cases}x_{n}^{-2}l_{2}^{3-b}(t)e^{-C_{0}\frac{(9C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{2}^{2}(t)}},&b<n-1\\
x_{n}^{-2}l_{2}^{4-n}(t)\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{|\tilde{x}|})\rangle e^{-C_{0}\frac{(9C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{2}^{2}(t)}},&b=n-1\\
x_{n}^{-2}|\tilde{x}|^{n-1-b}l_{2}^{4-n}(t)e^{-C_{0}\frac{(9C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{2}^{2}(t)}},&b>n-1\end{cases},&x_{n}>l_{2}(t),\end{cases}

where (
B.1
), (
B.2
) are utilized for the case
x_{n}>l_{2}(t)
;
for the case
|\tilde{x}|<x_{n}\leq l_{2}(t)
,
b=n-1
, we used
n>2
, (
B.7
), (
B.2
) to get

\displaystyle\bigg(\int_{C_{0}\frac{(9C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{l_{2}^{2}(t)}}^{1}+\int_{1}^{C_{0}\frac{(99C_{l}C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}}\bigg)e^{-r}r^{\frac{n}{2}-2}\langle\ln(\frac{C_{0}x_{n}^{2}}{r|\tilde{x}|^{2}})\rangle dr

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}-1}\int_{C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}}^{\frac{l_{2}^{2}(t)}{(9C_{*})^{2}|\tilde{x}|^{2}}}z^{-\frac{n}{2}}\langle\ln z\rangle dz+\langle\ln(\frac{x_{n}}{|\tilde{x}|})\rangle\sim\langle\ln(\frac{x_{n}}{|\tilde{x}|})\rangle.

For
u_{233}
, by (
B.1
), then

\displaystyle u_{233}\lesssim

\displaystyle|\tilde{x}|^{n-3-b}\int_{t-\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{(99C_{l}C_{*})^{2}}}^{t}(t-s)^{1-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\frac{|x|^{2}}{t-s}}ds=|\tilde{x}|^{n-3-b}\left(C_{0}|x|^{2}\right)^{2-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{C_{0}\frac{(99C_{l}C_{*})^{2}|x|^{2}}{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}}^{\infty}e^{-r}r^{\frac{n}{2}-3}dr

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|x|^{-2}|\tilde{x}|^{3-b}e^{-C_{0}\frac{(99C_{l}C_{*})^{2}|x|^{2}}{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}}\sim|x|^{-2}|\tilde{x}|^{3-b}e^{-C_{0}\frac{(99C_{l}C_{*})^{2}x_{n}^{2}}{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}}.

In sum, when
n>2
, for
2^{-1}C_{l}^{-1}l_{1}(t)<|\tilde{x}|\leq\min\left\{9C_{l}l_{2}(t),9C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}
,
through tedious comparison, we have

u_{2}\lesssim w_{22}:=\begin{cases}\begin{cases}l_{2}^{1-b}(t),&b<1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{|\tilde{x}|})\rangle,&b=1\\
|\tilde{x}|^{1-b},&1<b<n-1\\
|\tilde{x}|^{2-n}\langle\ln(\frac{|\tilde{x}|}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle,&b=n-1\\
|\tilde{x}|^{2-n}l_{1}^{n-1-b}(t),&b>n-1\end{cases},&x_{n}\leq|\tilde{x}|\\
\begin{cases}l_{2}^{1-b}(t),&b<1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{x_{n}})\rangle,&b=1\\
x_{n}^{1-b},&1<b<n-1\\
x_{n}^{2-n}\langle\ln(\frac{x_{n}}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle,&b=n-1\\
x_{n}^{2-n}l_{1}^{n-1-b}(t),&b>n-1\end{cases},&|\tilde{x}|<x_{n}\leq l_{2}(t)\\
\begin{cases}l_{2}^{n-1-b}(t),&b<n-1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle,&b=n-1\\
l_{1}^{n-1-b}(t),&b>n-1\end{cases}\begin{cases}x_{n}^{2-n},&l_{2}(t)<x_{n}\leq C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
x_{n}^{-2}t^{2-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-2C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{t}},&x_{n}>C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}},\end{cases}\end{cases}

where we used Lemma
B.1
(
3
) for the case
x_{n}>C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}
;
(
B.6
) for the case
x_{n}\leq|\tilde{x}|
,
b=n-1
, and the case
|\tilde{x}|<x_{n}\leq l_{2}(t)
,
b=n-1
.

For
|\tilde{x}|>\min\left\{9C_{l}l_{2}(t),9C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}
, we estimate
\tilde{u}_{2}
instead of
u_{2}
. For
\tilde{u}_{2}
, note that
|\tilde{x}-y|^{2}\geq\frac{64}{81}|\tilde{x}|^{2}
, then

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{2}\leq\int_{\frac{t}{2}}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-C_{0}\left(x_{n}^{2}+\frac{64}{81}|\tilde{x}|^{2}\right)\frac{1}{t-s}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}|y|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{C_{l}^{-1}l_{1}(t)\leq|y|\leq C_{l}l_{2}(t)}dyds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left[C_{0}\left(x_{n}^{2}+\frac{64}{81}|\tilde{x}|^{2}\right)\right]^{1-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{2C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}+\frac{64}{81}|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{t}}^{\infty}e^{-r}r^{\frac{n}{2}-2}dr\begin{cases}l_{2}^{n-1-b}(t),&b<n-1\\
\ln(C_{l}^{2}\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)}),&b=n-1\\
l_{1}^{n-1-b}(t),&b>n-1.\end{cases}

Since
n>2
, using (
B.1
) for the case
|x|>t^{\frac{1}{2}}
, we have

\tilde{u}_{2}\lesssim w_{23}:=\begin{cases}l_{2}^{n-1-b}(t),&b<n-1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle,&b=n-1\\
l_{1}^{n-1-b}(t),&b>n-1\end{cases}\begin{cases}|x|^{2-n},&|x|\leq t^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
|x|^{-2}t^{2-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-2C_{0}\frac{x_{n}^{2}+\frac{64}{81}|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{t}},&|x|>t^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{cases}

Since
w_{21}\sim w_{22}
when
|\tilde{x}|\sim l_{1}(t)
,
\min\left\{9C_{l}l_{2}(t),9C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}\sim l_{2}(t)
due to the assumption
l_{2}(t)\leq C_{*}t^{\frac{1}{2}}
, and
w_{22}\sim w_{23}
when
|\tilde{x}|\sim l_{2}(t)
, we get the first conclusion

\mathcal{T}\left[v(t)|x|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{l_{1}(t)\leq|x|\leq l_{2}(t)}\right]\lesssim w_{1}+\sup\limits_{t_{1}\in\left[\max\{t_{0},t/2\},t\right]}v(t_{1})\left(w_{21}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{1}(t)}+w_{22}\mathbf{1}_{l_{1}(t)<|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{2}(t)}+w_{23}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{x}|>l_{2}(t)}\right),

where
w_{21}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{1}(t)}+w_{22}\mathbf{1}_{l_{1}(t)<|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{2}(t)}+w_{23}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{x}|>l_{2}(t)}\sim w_{2}
.

The upper bound of
\mathcal{T}\left[v(t)\left(|\tilde{x}|+l_{1}(t)\right)^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{2}(t)}\right]
is deduced by the first conclusion and

v(t)\left(|\tilde{x}|+l_{1}(t)\right)^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{2}(t)}\sim v(t)l_{1}^{-b}(t)\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{1}(t)}+v(t)|\tilde{x}|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{l_{1}(t)\leq|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{2}(t)}.

∎

B.3.
Exponential decay estimates for Neumann boundary
t^{a}|\tilde{x}|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{l_{1}(t)\leq|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{2}(t)}
and
t^{a}\left(|\tilde{x}|+l_{1}(t)\right)^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{2}(t)}

Lemma B.3
.

Let
n>2
be an integer,
t>t_{0}\geq 1
,
x=(\tilde{x},x_{n})
,
\tilde{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
,
x_{n}\geq 0
. For an admissible function
f
, we define

\mathcal{T}_{1}\left[f\right](x,t):=2\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left[4\pi(t-s)\right]^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|\tilde{x}-y|^{2}+x_{n}^{2}}{4(t-s)}}f(y,s)dyds,

which satisfies the equation

{\partial}_{t}u=\Delta u\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}\times(t_{0},\infty),\quad-{\partial}_{x_{n}}u=f(\tilde{x},t)\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}\times(t_{0},\infty),\quad u(\cdot,t_{0})=0\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}.

Suppose that
0\leq l_{1}(s)\leq l_{2}(s)\leq C_{*}s^{\frac{1}{2}}
for
s\in[t_{0},t]
,

C_{l}^{-1}l_{1}(t)\leq l_{1}(s)
for all
s\in[\max\{t_{0},\frac{t}{2}\},t]
,
C_{l}^{-1}t^{p_{2}}\leq l_{2}(t)\leq C_{l}t^{p_{2}}
, where
C_{*}>0,C_{l}\geq 1
are constants,

b<n-1,\quad\begin{cases}p_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2},a+p_{2}(1-b)+\frac{n}{2}\geq 0,&\mbox{ \ if \ }a+p_{2}(n-1-b)\neq-1\\
p_{2}<\frac{1}{2},&\mbox{ \ if \ }a+p_{2}(n-1-b)=-1,\end{cases}

then

\mathcal{T}_{1}\left[t^{a}|\tilde{x}|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{l_{1}(t)\leq|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{2}(t)}\right](x,t)\leq Ct^{a}\begin{cases}\begin{cases}l_{2}^{1-b}(t),&b<1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle,&b=1\\
l_{1}^{1-b}(t),&b>1\end{cases},&|x|\leq l_{1}(t)\\
\begin{cases}l_{2}^{1-b}(t),&b<1\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{|x|})\rangle,&b=1\\
|x|^{1-b},&b>1\end{cases},&l_{1}(t)<|x|\leq l_{2}(t)\\
l_{2}^{1-b}(t)e^{-\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}+|x_{n}+1|^{2}}{4t}},&|x|>l_{2}(t)\end{cases}

with the convention
\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)}=1
if
l_{1}(t)=l_{2}(t)=0
, where
C
is a constant only depending on
n,a,b,p_{2},C_{*},C_{l}
.

Under the additional assumption that
l_{1}(s)\leq C_{l}l_{1}(t)
for all
s\in[\max\{t_{0},\frac{t}{2}\},t]
, the same conclusion is held for

\mathcal{T}_{1}\left[t^{a}\left(|\tilde{x}|+l_{1}(t)\right)^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{2}(t)}\right]
.

Proof.

By Lemma
B.2
, the upper bound estimate of
\mathcal{T}_{1}\left[f\right](x,t)
has two parts. For
b<n-1
,
p_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, in order to make the second part dominate the first part in the range
|x|\leq l_{2}(t)
,
it suffices to guarantee

t^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{t_{0}}^{\max\{t_{0},\frac{t}{2}\}}s^{a+p_{2}(n-1-b)}ds\lesssim t^{a+p_{2}(1-b)},

which can be deduced under the assumption

\begin{cases}p_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2},a+p_{2}(1-b)+\frac{n}{2}\geq 0,&a+p_{2}(n-1-b)\neq-1\\
p_{2}<\frac{1}{2},&a+p_{2}(n-1-b)=-1.\end{cases}

Then
\mathcal{T}_{1}\left[t^{a}|\tilde{x}|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{l_{1}(t)\leq|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{2}(t)}\right]\lesssim t^{a+p_{2}(1-b)}
on
|x|=l_{2}(t)
. Denote
f:=t^{a+p_{2}(1-b)}e^{-\kappa\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}+|x_{n}+1|^{2}}{t}}
.
Then

\left({\partial}_{t}-\Delta\right)f=e^{-\kappa\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}+|x_{n}+1|^{2}}{t}}t^{-1+a+p_{2}(1-b)}\left[\left(\kappa-4\kappa^{2}\right)t^{-1}\left(|\tilde{x}|^{2}+|x_{n}+1|^{2}\right)+2\kappa n+a+p_{2}(1-b)\right],

which is non-negative in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\left(t_{0},\infty\right)
if

\kappa\in[0,\frac{1}{4}]
and
2\kappa n+a+p_{2}(1-b)\geq 0
.

\left(-{\partial}_{x_{n}}f\right)\left(\tilde{x},0,t\right)=2\kappa t^{a+p_{2}(1-b)-1}e^{-\kappa\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}+1}{t}}.

Take
\kappa=\frac{1}{4}
. As a result,
Cf
is a barrier function in the range
|x|>l_{2}(t)
when
C
is sufficiently large and the first conclusion holds.

The pointwise upper bound of
\mathcal{T}_{1}\left[t^{a}\left(|\tilde{x}|+l_{1}(t)\right)^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{x}|\leq l_{2}(t)}\right]
is deduced similarly.

∎

B.4.
Neumann boundary value
t^{a}|\tilde{x}|^{-b}e^{-\ell\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{t}}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{x}|\geq t^{\frac{1}{2}}}

Lemma B.4
.

Let
n\geq 2
be an integer,
\ell>0
,
\kappa\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
,
\frac{1}{2}+a-\frac{b}{2}+2\kappa n\geq 0
,
t>t_{0}\geq 1
,
x=(\tilde{x},x_{n})
,
\tilde{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
,
x_{n}\geq 0
,
\mathcal{T}_{1}\left[\cdot\right]
defined in Lemma
B.3
, then

\mathcal{T}_{1}\left[t^{a}|\tilde{x}|^{-b}e^{-\ell\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{t}}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{x}|\geq t^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right](x,t)\leq t^{a-\frac{b}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\kappa\frac{|x|^{2}}{t}}\begin{cases}C(\kappa),&\mbox{ \ if \ }b\geq-1,\kappa\in\left(0,\ell\right]\cap(0,\frac{1}{4})\\
C(b,\ell,\kappa),&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<-1,\kappa\in(0,\min\{\ell,\frac{1}{4}\}),\end{cases}

where
C(\kappa)>0
is a constant depending on
\kappa
and
C(b,\ell,\kappa)>0
is a constant depending on
b,\ell,\kappa
.

Proof.

Set
f_{1}(x,t)=t^{a-\frac{b}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\kappa\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}+x_{n}^{2}}{t}}
,

f_{2}(x,t)=t^{a-\frac{b}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\kappa\frac{\left(|\tilde{x}|+x_{n}\right)^{2}}{t}}

with a constant
\kappa
to be determined later.
Direct calculation gives

\displaystyle\left({\partial}_{t}-\Delta\right)f_{1}=e^{-\kappa\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}+x_{n}^{2}}{t}}t^{a-\frac{b}{2}-\frac{3}{2}}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}+a-\frac{b}{2}+2\kappa n\right)t+\kappa\left(1-4\kappa\right)\left(|\tilde{x}|^{2}+x_{n}^{2}\right)\right],

\displaystyle\left(-{\partial}_{x_{n}}f_{1}\right)\left(\tilde{x},0,t\right)=0,

\displaystyle\left({\partial}_{t}-\Delta\right)f_{2}=e^{-\kappa\frac{\left(|\tilde{x}|+x_{n}\right)^{2}}{t}}t^{a-\frac{b}{2}-\frac{3}{2}}\left[\left(\frac{1}{2}+a-\frac{b}{2}+2\kappa n\right)t+2\kappa\left(n-2\right)t\frac{x_{n}}{|\tilde{x}|}+\kappa\left(1-8\kappa\right)\left(|\tilde{x}|+x_{n}\right)^{2}\right],

\displaystyle\left(-{\partial}_{x_{n}}f_{2}\right)\left(\tilde{x},0,t\right)=2\kappa t^{a-\frac{b}{2}-\frac{1}{2}}|\tilde{x}|e^{-\kappa\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{t}}.

Since
n\geq 2
,
\kappa\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
,
\frac{1}{2}+a-\frac{b}{2}+2\kappa n\geq 0
,
x_{n}\geq 0
, there exists
C_{2}(\kappa)>0
small such that
\left({\partial}_{t}-\Delta\right)\left(f_{1}+C_{2}(\kappa)f_{2}\right)\geq 0
. Additionally,

\displaystyle\left[-{\partial}_{x_{n}}\left(f_{1}+C_{2}(\kappa)f_{2}\right)\right]\left(\tilde{x},0,t\right)\geq C_{2}(\kappa)2\kappa t^{a-\frac{b}{2}-\frac{1}{2}}|\tilde{x}|e^{-\kappa\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{t}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle t^{a}|\tilde{x}|^{-b}e^{-\ell\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{t}}\mathbf{1}_{|\tilde{x}|\geq t^{\frac{1}{2}}}\begin{cases}C(\kappa),&\mbox{ \ if \ }b\geq-1,\kappa\in(0,\ell]\\
C(b,\ell,\kappa),&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<-1,\kappa\in(0,\ell)\end{cases}

for some positive constants
C(\kappa),C(b,\ell,\kappa)
.
Thus,
C\left(f_{1}+C_{2}(\kappa)f_{2}\right)
is a barrier function with
C
sufficiently large.

∎

B.5.
Right-hand sides
t^{a}|x|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{l_{1}(t)\leq|x|\leq l_{2}(t)}
and
t^{a}\left(|x|+l_{1}(t)\right)^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{|x|\leq l_{2}(t)}

Lemma B.5
.

Let
n>2
be an integer,
t>t_{0}\geq 1
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For an admissible function
f
, we define

(B.8)

\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left[f\right](x,t):=\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left[4\pi(t-s)\right]^{-\frac{n}{2}}e^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{4(t-s)}}f(y,s)dyds,

which satisfies the equation
{\partial}_{t}u=\Delta u+f
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times(t_{0},\infty)
,

u(\cdot,t_{0})=0

in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Suppose that
0\leq l_{1}(s)\leq l_{2}(s)\leq C_{*}s^{\frac{1}{2}}
for
s\in[t_{0},t]
,

C_{l}^{-1}l_{1}(t)\leq l_{1}(s)
for all
s\in[\max\{t_{0},\frac{t}{2}\},t]
,
C_{l}^{-1}t^{p_{2}}\leq l_{2}(t)\leq C_{l}t^{p_{2}}
, where
C_{*}>0,C_{l}\geq 1
are constants,

b<n,\quad\begin{cases}p_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2},a+p_{2}(2-b)+\frac{n}{2}\geq 0,&\mbox{ \ if \ }a+p_{2}(n-b)\neq-1\\
p_{2}<\frac{1}{2},&\mbox{ \ if \ }a+p_{2}(n-b)=-1,\end{cases}

then

\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left[t^{a}|x|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{l_{1}(t)\leq|x|\leq l_{2}(t)}\right](x,t)\leq Ct^{a}\begin{cases}\begin{cases}l_{2}^{2-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<2\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=2\\
l_{1}^{2-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>2\end{cases},&|x|\leq l_{1}(t)\\
\begin{cases}l_{2}^{2-b}(t)&\mbox{ \ if \ }b<2\\
\langle\ln(\frac{l_{2}(t)}{|x|})\rangle&\mbox{ \ if \ }b=2\\
|x|^{2-b}&\mbox{ \ if \ }b>2\end{cases},&l_{1}(t)<|x|\leq l_{2}(t)\\
l_{2}^{2-b}(t)e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{4t}},&|x|>l_{2}(t)\end{cases}

with the convention
\frac{l_{2}(t)}{l_{1}(t)}=1
if
l_{1}(t)=l_{2}(t)=0
, where
C
is a constant only depending on
n,a,b,p_{2},C_{*},C_{l}
.

Under the additional assumption that
l_{1}(s)\leq C_{l}l_{1}(t)
for all
s\in[\max\{t_{0},\frac{t}{2}\},t]
, the same conclusion is held for

\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left[t^{a}\left(|x|+l_{1}(t)\right)^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{|x|\leq l_{2}(t)}\right]
.

Proof.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
B.2
, the requirement “
C_{l}^{-1}l_{i}(t)\leq l_{i}(s)\leq C_{l}l_{i}(t)
,
i=1,2
, for all
\frac{t}{2}\leq s\leq t
,
t\geq t_{0}
” in
[
59
, Lemma A.1]
can be relaxed to “
C_{l}^{-1}l_{1}(t)\leq l_{1}(s)
, and
l_{2}(s)\leq C_{l}l_{2}(t)
, for all
s\in[\max\{t_{0},\frac{t}{2}\},t]
” and the “
\lesssim
” in
[
59
, Lemma A.1]
is indeed independent of
t_{0}
.

One recalling
[
59
, Lemma A.1]
, the estimate of
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left[t^{a}|x|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{l_{1}(t)\leq|x|\leq l_{2}(t)}\right]
is split into two parts. We call the term including
\int_{t_{0}/2}^{t/2}
as the first part and the other term as the second part. For
b<n
,
p_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, in order to make the second part dominate the first part in the range
|x|\leq l_{2}(t)
,
it suffices to make

t^{-\frac{n}{2}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t/2}s^{a+p_{2}(n-b)}ds\leq Ct^{a+p_{2}(2-b)}

with a constant
C
independent of
t_{0}
. This can be deduced under the assumption

\begin{cases}p_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2},a+p_{2}(2-b)+\frac{n}{2}\geq 0,&\mbox{ \ if \ }a+p_{2}(n-b)\neq-1\\
p_{2}<\frac{1}{2},&\mbox{ \ if \ }a+p_{2}(n-b)=-1.\end{cases}

Then
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left[t^{a}|x|^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{l_{1}(t)\leq|x|\leq l_{2}(t)}\right]\lesssim t^{a+p_{2}(2-b)}
on
|x|=l_{2}(t)
. Denote
f:=t^{a+p_{2}(2-b)}e^{-\kappa\frac{|x|^{2}}{t}}
with a constant
\kappa
to be determined later. Then

\left({\partial}_{t}-\Delta\right)f=e^{-\kappa\frac{|x|^{2}}{t}}t^{-1+a+p_{2}(2-b)}\left[\left(\kappa-4\kappa^{2}\right)t^{-1}|x|^{2}+2\kappa n+a+p_{2}(2-b)\right],

which is non-negative in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\left(t_{0},\infty\right)
if

\kappa\in[0,\frac{1}{4}]
and
2\kappa n+a+p_{2}(2-b)\geq 0
. For
b<n
,
p_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, we take
\kappa=\frac{1}{4}
and
Cf
is a barrier function in the range
|x|>l_{2}(t)
when
C
is sufficiently large.

The estimate about
\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left[t^{a}\left(|x|+l_{1}(t)\right)^{-b}\mathbf{1}_{|x|\leq l_{2}(t)}\right]
is similar.
∎

Appendix C
Sobolev-type lemmas

Lemma C.1
.

Given a domain
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
(possibly unbounded), suppose that

2\leq p<\infty
if
n=1,2
,
2\leq p\leq\frac{2n}{n-2}
if
n\geq 3
, and a sequence
(u_{k})_{k\geq 1}\subset H^{1}(\Omega)
satisfies

u_{k}\rightharpoonup v_{1}\mbox{ \ in \ }H^{1}(\Omega),\quad\nabla u_{k}\rightharpoonup\bm{v_{2}}=(v_{21},v_{22},\dots,v_{2n})\mbox{ \ in \ }L^{2}(\Omega),\quad u_{k}\rightharpoonup v_{3}\mbox{ \ in \ }L^{p}(\Omega),

then
\nabla v_{1}=\bm{v_{2}}
in
L^{2}(\Omega)
,
v_{1}=v_{3}
in
L^{p}(\Omega)
.

Proof.

Given a function
f\in L^{2}(\Omega)
,
\int_{\Omega}f{\partial}_{x_{i}}u_{k}\rightarrow\int_{\Omega}fv_{2i}
,
i=1,2,\dots,n
.
For any
g\in H^{1}(\Omega)
,
g\mapsto\int_{\Omega}f{\partial}_{x_{i}}g

is a bounded linear mapping on
H^{1}(\Omega)
. By
u_{k}\rightharpoonup v_{1}
in
H^{1}(\Omega)
, we have

\int_{\Omega}f{\partial}_{x_{i}}u_{k}\rightarrow\int_{\Omega}f{\partial}_{x_{i}}v_{1}
.
Due to the arbitrary choice of
f\in L^{2}(\Omega)
, then
{\partial}_{x_{i}}v_{1}=v_{2i}
in
L^{2}(\Omega)
.

For the other part, given a function
f_{1}\in L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega)
, where
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}=1
,
\int_{\Omega}f_{1}u_{k}\rightarrow\int_{\Omega}f_{1}v_{3}
.
For any
g\in H^{1}(\Omega)
,
g\mapsto\int_{\Omega}f_{1}g

is also a bounded linear mapping on
H^{1}(\Omega)
by the Sobolev embedding theorem and the choice of
p
. By
u_{k}\rightharpoonup v_{1}
in
H^{1}(\Omega)
, we have
\int_{\Omega}f_{1}u_{k}\rightarrow\int_{\Omega}f_{1}v_{1}
.
Due to the arbitrary choice of
f_{1}\in L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega)
, then
v_{1}=v_{3}
in
L^{p}(\Omega)
.
∎

Lemma C.2
.

Let
V(\tilde{x})\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})
,
V(\tilde{x})\to 0
as
|\tilde{x}|\to\infty
. If a sequence
(u_{k})_{k\geq 1}
satisfies

u_{k}\rightharpoonup u_{0}
in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
as
k\to\infty
, then up to a subsequence,
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}|V(\tilde{x})|\left|(u_{k}-u_{0})(\tilde{x},0)\right|^{2}d\tilde{x}\to 0
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}V(\tilde{x})\left|u_{k}(\tilde{x},0)\right|^{2}d\tilde{x}\to\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}V(\tilde{x})\left|u_{0}(\tilde{x},0)\right|^{2}d\tilde{x}
as
k\to\infty
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, assume
u_{k}\rightharpoonup 0
.
Since the Sobolev embedding
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})\hookrightarrow L^{2}({\partial}\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
is continuous, we have
\sup_{k\geq 0}\|u_{k}(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})}\leq C
. Thus, for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
R
sufficiently large such that

\int_{|\tilde{x}|\geq R}\left|V(\tilde{x})\right|\left|u_{k}(\tilde{x},0)\right|^{2}d\tilde{x}

<\epsilon
.
Since for any compact set
\Omega\subset{\partial}\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
, the Sobolev embedding
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})\hookrightarrow L^{2}(\Omega)
is compact, up to a subsequence,
\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}\int_{|\tilde{x}|<R}V(\tilde{x})\left|u_{k}(\tilde{x},0)\right|^{2}d\tilde{x}=0
. Thus,
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}|V(\tilde{x})|\left|(u_{k}-u_{0})(\tilde{x},0)\right|^{2}d\tilde{x}\to 0
as
k\to\infty
. Combining
\sup_{k\geq 0}
\|u_{k}(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})}

\leq C
, we have the second convergence result.
∎

Recall
L^{2}_{\rho}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1}),H^{1}_{\rho}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
defined in (
2.4
), (
2.5
) respectively.

Lemma C.3
.

Given an integer
n\geq 2
, for
u\in H^{1}_{\rho}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}u^{2}(\tilde{x},0)e^{-\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{4}}d\tilde{x}\leq\frac{n+4}{4}\|u\|_{H_{\rho}^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}^{2}
.

Proof.

Set the norm

\|f\|_{H_{V}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}:=\big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}(|\nabla f|^{2}+V(x)f^{2})dx\big]^{1/2}
with
V(x):=\frac{|x|^{2}}{16}+1
.
For any
u\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}})
, let
u(x)=e^{\frac{|x|^{2}}{8}}v(x)
, then direct calculation gives that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|u|^{2}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{4}}dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}v^{2}dx,\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|\nabla u|^{2}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{4}}dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\left[|\nabla v|^{2}+\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{16}-\frac{n}{4}\right)v^{2}\right]dx,

\displaystyle\|v\|_{H_{V}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\left(|\nabla u|^{2}+\frac{n+4}{4}u^{2}\right)e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{4}}dx,

which indicates

\|u\|_{H_{\rho}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}^{2}\leq\|v\|_{H_{V}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}^{2}\leq\frac{n+4}{4}\|u\|_{H_{\rho}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}^{2}
.
Thus,

(C.1)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}u^{2}(\tilde{x},0)e^{-\frac{|\tilde{x}|^{2}}{4}}d\tilde{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}v^{2}(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}{\partial}_{x_{n}}(v^{2})dx=-2\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}v{\partial}_{x_{n}}vdx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\|v\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}\|{\partial}_{x_{n}}v\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}\leq\ \|v\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}^{2}+\|\nabla v\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}^{2}\leq\frac{n+4}{4}\|u\|_{H_{\rho}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}^{2},

which implies the lemma by approximating general functions in
H^{1}_{\rho}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
by
C_{c}^{\infty}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}})
.
∎

Appendix D
Negative eigenvalue and exponential decay of the eigenfunction

This section is devoted to the eigenvalue problem about the linearized equation around the ground state
U(x)
. The main result is Proposition
D.5
.

Define

D^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
as the completion of
C_{c}^{\infty}(\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}})
(
f(\tilde{x},0)
can be nonzero) under the norm
\left\|\nabla f\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}
. Obviously,
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})\subsetneqq D^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
.
Given an integer
n\geq 3
,
p=\frac{n}{n-2}
,
for
v,f,g\in D^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
, set functionals

(D.1)

\displaystyle J[v]:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\frac{\left|\nabla v\right|^{2}}{2}dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\frac{\left|v\right|^{p+1}(\tilde{x},0)}{p+1}d\tilde{x},\quad I[v]:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\left|\nabla v\right|^{2}dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left|v\right|^{p+1}(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x},

\displaystyle B_{v}[f,g]:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\nabla f\cdot\nabla gdx-p\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left(|v|^{p-1}fg\right)(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}.

Direct calculation deduces that
J[\cdot],I[\cdot]\in C^{2}\left(D^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}),\mathbb{R}\right)
and
I[v]=\langle J^{\prime}[v],v\rangle
,
B_{v}[f,g]:=\langle J^{\prime\prime}[v]f,g\rangle
.
Define

(D.2)

\displaystyle D_{*}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}):=\left\{f\in D^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})\ |\ f(\tilde{x},0)\in L^{p+1}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})\backslash\{0\}\right\},

\displaystyle Q(f):=\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}|f|^{p+1}(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}\Big)^{-\frac{2}{p+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}|\nabla f|^{2}dx,\quad f\in D_{*}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}).

Define the Nehari manifold

(D.3)

\mathbf{Ne}:=\left\{f\in D^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})\backslash\{0\}\ |\ I[f]=0\right\}.

By
[
16
, Theorem 1]
,
for all

f\in D^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
,

(D.4)

(n-2)^{\frac{1}{2}}2^{-\frac{1}{2}}|S^{n-1}|^{\frac{1}{2(n-1)}}\|f(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{p+1}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})}\leq\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}\quad\mbox{ \ with \ }p+1=\frac{2(n-1)}{n-2},\ n\geq 3,

where
|S^{n-1}|
is the volume of the
n-1
dimensional unit sphere.
The equality sign is attained only by

(D.5)

\varphi(x)=c_{1}c_{2}^{\frac{n-2}{2}}\left[|\tilde{x}-\tilde{v}|^{2}+\left(x_{n}+c_{2}\right)^{2}\right]^{-\frac{n-2}{2}},\quad x=(\tilde{x},x_{n})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}

with constants

c_{1}\neq 0
,
c_{2}>0
, and a constant vector
\tilde{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
. It follows that (
D.5
) attains
\inf\limits_{f\in D_{*}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}Q(f)
.
By (
D.4
), we have

(D.6)

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\left|\nabla f\right|^{2}dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left|f\right|^{p+1}(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}\geq\big[(n-2)^{\frac{1}{2}}2^{-\frac{1}{2}}|S^{n-1}|^{\frac{1}{2(n-1)}}\big]^{\frac{2(p+1)}{p-1}}\mbox{ \ for \ }f\in\mathbf{Ne};\quad\mathbf{Ne}\subsetneqq D_{*}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}).

Define the tangent space at
v\in\mathbf{Ne}
as

(D.7)

T_{\mathbf{Ne}}v:=\Big\{f\in D^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})\ |\ \langle I^{\prime}[v],f\rangle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\nabla v\cdot\nabla fdx-\left(p+1\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left(\left|v\right|^{p-1}vf\right)(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}=0\Big\}.

Lemma D.1
.

Suppose that
n\geq 3
is an integer,
p=\frac{n}{n-2}
, and
v\in\mathbf{Ne}
attains
\inf\limits_{f\in\mathbf{Ne}}J[f]\in\mathbb{R}
, then

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\nabla v\cdot\nabla gdx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left(|v|^{p-1}vg\right)(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}=0\quad\mbox{ \ for \ }g\in D^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n});\quad B_{v}[\varphi,\varphi]\geq 0\quad\mbox{ \ for \ }\varphi\in T_{\mathbf{Ne}}v.

Proof.

The first result is deduced by the Lagrange multiplier method.
For the second result, there exists
\psi\in D^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
such that
\langle I^{\prime}[v],\psi\rangle\neq 0
. Indeed, since
v\in\mathbf{Ne}
and
\langle I^{\prime}[v],v\rangle=\left(1-p\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left|v\right|^{p+1}(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}\neq 0
, we can take
\psi=v
.
Given
\varphi\in T_{\mathbf{Ne}}v
, set
H(t,s):=I\left[v+t\varphi+s\psi\right]
.
Then
H(0,0)=0
and
{\partial}_{s}H(t,s)\big|_{(t,s)=(0,0)}=\langle I^{\prime}[v],\psi\rangle\neq 0
. For
|t|,|s|
sufficiently small,
H(t,s)
is smooth about
t,s
.
By the implicit function theorem, there exists
\delta>0
small such that

H\left(t,s(t)\right)\equiv 0\quad\forall t\in(-\delta,\delta);\quad s=s(t)\in C^{2}(-\delta,\delta);\quad s(0)=0;\quad s^{\prime}(0)=-\frac{\langle I^{\prime}[v],\varphi\rangle}{\langle I^{\prime}[v],\psi\rangle}=0.

Set
h(t):=t\varphi+s(t)\psi
, and then
h(t)
satisfies

h(0)=0,\quad h^{\prime}(0)=\varphi,\quad h(t)\in C^{2}((-\delta,\delta),D^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})),\quad v+h(t)\in\mathbf{Ne}\mbox{ \ for \ }t\in(-\delta,\delta),0<\delta\ll 1,

where we take
\delta\ll 1
to make
v+h(t)\neq 0
.
The fact that
v\in\mathbf{Ne}
attains
\inf\limits_{f\in\mathbf{Ne}}J[f]
implies
\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}J(v+h(t))\big|_{t=0}\geq 0
. Therein,

\displaystyle\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}J(v+h(t))\Big|_{t=0}=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\left|\nabla h^{\prime}(0)\right|^{2}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\nabla v\cdot\nabla h^{\prime\prime}(0)dx-p\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left(\left|v\right|^{p-1}\left(h^{\prime}(0)\right)^{2}\right)(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left(\left|v\right|^{p-1}vh^{\prime\prime}(0)\right)(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}=B_{v}[\varphi,\varphi],

where we used the first result for the last step. Hence the second result holds.
∎

Lemma D.2
.

Assume that
n\geq 3
is an integer,
p=\frac{n}{n-2}
.
For
f\in\mathbf{Ne}
, we have

(D.8)

J[f]=\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p+1}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\left|\nabla f\right|^{2}dx=\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p+1}\right)\left(Q(f)\right)^{\frac{p+1}{p-1}}.

Given
f\in D_{*}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
, for a constant
c_{f}>0
,

(D.9)

\displaystyle I[c_{f}f]=c_{f}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\left|\nabla f\right|^{2}dx-c_{f}^{p+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left|f\right|^{p+1}(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}=0\Leftrightarrow c_{f}=\Bigg(\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\left|\nabla f\right|^{2}dx}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left|f\right|^{p+1}(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{p-1}},

\displaystyle\mbox{and then \ }c_{f}f\in\mathbf{Ne},\quad J[c_{f}f]=\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p+1}\right)\left(Q(f)\right)^{\frac{p+1}{p-1}}.

Moreover,

\inf\limits_{f\in\mathbf{Ne}}J[f]=\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p+1}\right)\Big(\inf\limits_{f\in D_{*}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}Q(f)\Big)^{\frac{p+1}{p-1}}.

In particular, if
f_{1}\in\mathbf{Ne}
attains
\inf\limits_{f\in\mathbf{Ne}}J[f]
, then
f_{1}
attains
\inf\limits_{f\in D_{*}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}Q(f)
;
conversely, if
f_{2}\in D_{*}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
attains
\inf\limits_{f\in D_{*}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}Q(f)
, then
c_{f_{2}}f_{2}\in\mathbf{Ne}
and
c_{f_{2}}f_{2}
attains
\inf\limits_{f\in\mathbf{Ne}}J[f]
.

Proof.

(
D.8
) and (
D.6
) imply

\inf\limits_{f\in\mathbf{Ne}}J[f]=\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p+1}\right)\Big(\inf\limits_{f\in\mathbf{Ne}}Q(f)\Big)^{\frac{p+1}{p-1}}\geq\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p+1}\right)\Big(\inf\limits_{f\in D_{*}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}Q(f)\Big)^{\frac{p+1}{p-1}}.

Given
f\in D_{*}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
, the choice of
c_{f}
yields
c_{f}f\in\mathbf{Ne}
. One combining (
D.8
), then

J[c_{f}f]=\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p+1}\right)\left(Q(f)\right)^{\frac{p+1}{p-1}}
,
which implies

\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p+1}\right)\Big(\inf\limits_{f\in D_{*}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}Q(f)\Big)^{\frac{p+1}{p-1}}=\inf\limits_{f\in D_{*}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}J[c_{f}f]\geq\inf\limits_{c_{f}f\in\mathbf{Ne}}J[c_{f}f]\geq\inf\limits_{f\in\mathbf{Ne}}J[f].

∎

Lemma D.3
.

Given an integer
n\geq 2
and constants
\lambda=-m^{2}
,
m>0
, then for
u\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})\cap C^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}})
and
x\in\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}
, we have

(D.10)

\displaystyle u(x)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\left(\Delta u+\lambda u\right)(y)\left[-E_{n}^{m}(|x-y|)-E_{n}^{m}(|x-(\tilde{y},-y_{n})|)\right]dy

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}(-\partial_{y_{n}}u)(\tilde{y},0)\left[-2E_{n}^{m}(|x-(\tilde{y},0)|)\right]d\tilde{y},

where
E_{n}^{m}(r)=m^{\frac{n}{2}-1}(2\pi)^{-\frac{n}{2}}r^{1-\frac{n}{2}}K_{\frac{n}{2}-1}(mr)
for
r>0
and
K_{\frac{n}{2}-1}
is the second kind modified Bessel function of order
\frac{n}{2}-1
. Moreover,

(D.11)

\left|E_{n}^{m}(r)\right|\leq C(m,n)\bigg(\mathbf{1}_{r<1}\begin{cases}r^{2-n},&n>2\\
\langle\ln r\rangle,&n=2\end{cases}+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq 1}e^{-mr}\bigg)\quad\mbox{ \ for \ }r>0.

Remark D.3.1
.

Similar to (
2.17
), (
D.10
) can be used to represent solutions with some rough data.

Proof.

By
[
55
, Theorem 2.5]
, the fundamental solution of
\Delta+\lambda
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
is given by
-E_{n}^{m}(|x|)
, namely
\left(\Delta+\lambda\right)(-E_{n}^{m}(|x|))=\delta(x)
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. The properties of
-E_{n}^{m}
are given in
[
55
, pp.9-10]
.

Given
\tilde{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
,
x_{n}>0
,
x=(\tilde{x},x_{n})
,
take
v(y)=-E_{n}^{m}(|x-y|)-E_{n}^{m}(|x-(\tilde{y},-y_{n})|)
.
Direct calculation yields
\partial_{y_{n}}v\big|_{y_{n}=0}=0
,

\left(\Delta+\lambda\right)v=\left(\Delta_{y}+\lambda\right)\left[-E_{n}^{m}(|y-x|)-E_{n}^{m}\big(|y-(\tilde{x},-x_{n})|\big)\right]=\delta_{y-x}+\delta_{y-(\tilde{x},-x_{n})}
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Integration by parts yields

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\left(\Delta u+\lambda u\right)vdy=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left[(-\partial_{y_{n}}u)v-(-\partial_{y_{n}}v)u\right](\tilde{y},0)d\tilde{y}+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}u\left(\Delta v+\lambda v\right)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left[(-\partial_{y_{n}}u)v\right](\tilde{y},0)d\tilde{y}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(u(y)\mathbf{1}_{y_{n}>0}+0\mathbf{1}_{y_{n}\leq 0}\right)\left(\delta_{y-x}+\delta_{y-(\tilde{x},-x_{n})}\right)dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left[(-\partial_{y_{n}}u)v\right](\tilde{y},0)d\tilde{y}+u(x).

Taking
x_{n}\downarrow 0
deduces
the case
x\in\partial\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}
.
Plugging
v(y)
, then we complete the proof.
∎

Given
V_{1}(x)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
and
V_{2}(\tilde{x})\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})
, we say that
f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
satisfies the equation

-\Delta f=V_{1}(x)f\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n},\quad-\partial_{x_{n}}f=V_{2}(\tilde{x})f\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}

in the weak sense if

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\nabla f\cdot\nabla gdx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}V_{2}(\tilde{x})(fg)(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}V_{1}(x)fgdx\quad\mbox{ \ holds for all \ }g\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}).

Lemma D.4
.

Suppose that
n\geq 2
is an integer,
\lambda<0
,
V(\tilde{x})
satisfies

V(\tilde{x})\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})
and
\lim_{|\tilde{x}|\to\infty}V(\tilde{x})=0
,
let
\phi\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
satisfy

(D.12)

-\Delta\phi=\lambda\phi\quad\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+},\quad-{\partial}_{x_{n}}\phi=V(\tilde{x})\phi\quad\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}

in the weak sense.
Then for all
\nu\in[0,\sqrt{-\lambda})
,
we have
|\phi(x)|\leq Ce^{-\nu|x|}
in
\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}
with a constant
C
depending on
n,\lambda,\nu,\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})},V(\tilde{x})
.

Proof.

Denote
m=\sqrt{-\lambda}
. Since
\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}<\infty
, by
[
38
, Theorem 5.36, Theorem 5.45]
,
\phi\in C(\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}})
and
\left|\phi(x)\right|\to 0
as
|x|\to\infty
.
By the representation formula (
D.10
) in Lemma
D.3
and uniqueness of the weak solution of (
D.12
) in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
,
\phi
can be written as

(D.13)

\phi(x)=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}V(w)\phi(w,0)E_{n}^{m}(|x-(w,0)|)dw,

where
E_{n}^{m}
is given in Lemma
D.3
and satisfies (
D.11
).

The following argument is in the same spirit of
[
28
, Proof of Theorem 2.1]
. Given
\nu\in[0,m)
, denote

A(x):=\sup\limits_{w\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left|\phi(w,0)\right|e^{-\nu\left|x-(w,0)\right|},\quad B(x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}2\left|E_{n}^{m}(|x-(w,0)|)\right|e^{\nu\left|x-(w,0)\right|}\left|V(w)\right|dw.

If
A(x)=0
for some
x\in\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}
, then
\phi(\cdot,0)\equiv 0
in
\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
, which implies that
\phi\equiv 0
in
\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}
by (
D.13
) and the conclusion holds. Hereafter, we always assume
A(x)>0
in
\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}
.
Obviously,
\left|\phi(x)\right|\leq A(x)B(x)
. By (
D.11
),
\nu<m
, the properties of
V(x)
, and Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem,

B(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}2\left|E_{n}^{m}(|(z,x_{n})|)\right|e^{\nu\left|(z,x_{n})\right|}\left|V(\tilde{x}-z)\right|dz\to 0\mbox{ \ as \ }|x|\to\infty,

which implies that there exists
R_{1}=R_{1}(n,m,V(\tilde{x}))>0
sufficiently large such that

(D.14)

\left|\phi(x)\right|\leq 2^{-1}A(x)\quad\mbox{ \ for \ }x\in\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}},\ \left|x\right|\geq R_{1}.

Note that for
\nu\geq 0
,

\displaystyle A(x)=

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{w\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left|\phi(w,0)\right|\sup\limits_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}e^{-\nu\left|x-(z,0)\right|}e^{-\nu\left|(z,0)-(w,0)\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\sup\limits_{w\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left|\phi(w,0)\right|e^{-\nu\left|x-(z,0)\right|}e^{-\nu\left|(z,0)-(w,0)\right|}=\sup\limits_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}A(z,0)e^{-\nu\left|x-(z,0)\right|}.

Combining (
D.14
), we have

\sup\limits_{|w|>R_{1}}\left|\phi(w,0)\right|e^{-\nu\left|x-(w,0)\right|}\leq 2^{-1}\sup\limits_{|w|>R_{1}}A(w,0)e^{-\nu\left|x-(w,0)\right|}\leq 2^{-1}A(x)<A(x).

By the definition of
A(x)
, it implies

A(x)=\sup\limits_{|w|\leq R_{1}}\left|\phi(w,0)\right|e^{-\nu\left|x-(w,0)\right|}\leq e^{-\nu\left|x\right|}\sup\limits_{|w|\leq R_{1}}\left|\phi(w,0)\right|e^{\nu\left|w\right|}.

Combining (
D.14
) and the local
L^{\infty}
estimate
[
38
, Theorem 5.36]
in
\overline{B_{n}^{+}(0,2R_{1})}
, we conclude this lemma.
∎

Proposition D.5
.

Given an integer
n\geq 3
,
p=\frac{n}{n-2}
, and
U(x)
given in (
1.13
), there exists only one negative eigenvalue
\lambda_{0}
for the following eigenvalue problem in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
,

(D.15)

-\Delta f=\lambda_{0}f\mbox{ \ in \ }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n},\quad-\partial_{x_{n}}f=pU^{p-1}f\mbox{ \ on \ }{\partial}\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}.

The eigenvalue
\lambda_{0}
is simple with an eigenfunction
Z_{0}(x)\in C^{\infty}(\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}})\cap H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
satisfying
\|Z_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}=1
,

0<Z_{0}(x)\leq Ce^{-\nu|x|}
in
\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}
for all
\nu\in\left[0,\sqrt{-\lambda_{0}}\right)

with a constant
C
depending on
n,\lambda_{0},\nu
.
Moreover,

\lambda_{0}=\inf\limits_{0\not\equiv f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}^{-2}B_{U}[f,f]
.

Proof.

Step 1.

For any
f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
satisfying (
D.15
) in the weak sense, by parabolic regularity theorem,
f\in C^{\infty}(\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}})
.
By Lemma
D.4
,
for all
\nu\in\left[0,\sqrt{-\lambda_{0}}\right)
,
we have
|f(x)|\leq Ce^{-\nu|x|}
in
\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}
with a constant
C
depending on
n,\lambda_{0},\nu,\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}
.

Step 2.
Denote
\lambda_{*}=\inf\limits_{0\not\equiv f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}^{-2}B_{U}[f,f]
, where
B_{U}[f,f]
is well-defined since
U(x)\in D^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
for
n>2
.
Notice that

B_{U}[U,U]=\left(1-p\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}U^{p+1}(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}<0
. Since
U\not\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
when
n\leq 4
, instead, applying
\|U\eta(x/R)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}^{-2}B_{U}[U\eta(x/R),U\eta(x/R)]
with
R
sufficiently large implies

\lambda_{*}<0
.
For any
f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}f^{2}(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}\leq 2\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}\|{\partial}_{x_{n}}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}
,
then for any
\epsilon>0
,

p\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left(U^{p-1}f^{2}\right)(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}\leq\epsilon\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}^{2}+C(\epsilon)\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}^{2},

which implies
\lambda_{*}>-\infty
.

Step 3.

Take a sequence
(f_{k})_{k\geq 1}\subset H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
such that
\|f_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}=1
and
B_{U}[f_{k},f_{k}]=\lambda_{*}+o(1)
with
o(1)\to 0
as
k\to\infty
. It implies

\epsilon\|\nabla f_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}^{2}+C(\epsilon)\|f_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}^{2}\geq p\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left(U^{p-1}f_{k}^{2}\right)(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}\left|\nabla f_{k}\right|^{2}dx-\lambda_{*}+o(1)\geq-\frac{\lambda_{*}}{2}>0

when
k
is sufficiently large.
Thus

\left(1-\epsilon\right)\|\nabla f_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}^{2}\leq C(\epsilon)+\lambda_{*}+o(1),\quad p\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left(U^{p-1}f_{k}^{2}\right)(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}\geq-\frac{\lambda_{*}}{2}.

It follows that
\sup\limits_{k\geq 1}\|f_{k}\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}<\infty
. By Lemma
C.1
,
C.2
, up to a subsequence,

\displaystyle f_{k}\rightharpoonup f_{*}\mbox{ \ in \ }H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}),\quad\nabla f_{k}\rightharpoonup\nabla f_{*}\mbox{ \ in \ }L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}),\quad f_{k}\rightharpoonup f_{*}\mbox{ \ in \ }L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}),

\displaystyle p\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left(U^{p-1}f_{k}^{2}\right)(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}\to p\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\left(U^{p-1}f_{*}^{2}\right)(\tilde{x},0)d\tilde{x}\geq-\frac{\lambda_{*}}{2},

which implies

B_{U}[f_{*},f_{*}]\leq\lambda_{*}<0,\quad 0<\|f_{*}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}\leq 1

and then

\|f_{*}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}^{-2}B_{U}[f_{*},f_{*}]\leq B_{U}[f_{*},f_{*}]\leq\lambda_{*}.

By the definition of
\lambda_{*}
, we know that
\|f_{*}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}^{-2}B_{U}[f_{*},f_{*}]\geq\lambda_{*}
. Thus
\|f_{*}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}=1
and
B_{U}[f_{*},f_{*}]=\lambda_{*}
. Since
|\nabla|f||\leq|\nabla f|
a.e., by the definition of
\lambda_{*}
, we also have
B_{U}[|f_{*}|,|f_{*}|]=\lambda_{*}
.

Denote
S_{<}:=\big\{f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})\ |\

f
satisfies (
D.15
) with some
\lambda_{0}<0
in the weak sense
\big\}
. By the Lagrange multiplier method,
f_{*},|f_{*}|\in S_{<}
with the eigenvalue
\lambda_{*}
. Step 1 shows that the elements in
S_{<}
are smooth with exponential decay. By strong maximum principle and Hopf theorem,
|f_{*}|>0
in
\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}
.

Step 4.

Claim: the dimension of
S_{<}
is
1
.

Assume the opposite that there exists two linearly independent functions
f_{1},f_{2}\in S_{<}
with negative eigenvalues
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}
respectively.
Note that
\lambda_{1}(f_{1},f_{2})_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}=B_{U}[f_{1},f_{2}]=\lambda_{2}(f_{1},f_{2})_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}
. We can assume
(f_{1},f_{2})_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}=0
since if
\lambda_{1}\neq\lambda_{2}
,
it holds automatically, and if
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}
,
we replace
f_{1}
by
f_{1}-\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}^{-2}(f_{1},f_{2})_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}f_{2}\not\equiv 0
by the linear independence of
f_{1},f_{2}
. Then

B_{U}[f_{1},f_{2}]=0
. For
i=1,2
,
f_{i}\neq 0
deduces
B_{U}[f_{i},f_{i}]=\lambda_{i}\|f_{i}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})}^{2}<0
.

Since
J[\cdot]\in C^{2}\left(D^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}),\mathbb{R}\right)
,
\langle J^{\prime\prime}[g]g,g\rangle=(1-p)\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n}}|\nabla g|^{2}dx\neq 0
for
g\in\mathbf{Ne}
, and (
D.6
) holds,
by
[
43
, Proposition 5.75]
,
\mathbf{Ne}
is a complete
C^{1}
-Banach submanifold of
D^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
of
codimension
1
. Since
U(x)\in\mathbf{Ne}
for
n>2
, in particular,

T_{\mathbf{Ne}}U
is codimension
1
in
D^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})
.

Thus, there exists
w_{0}\in D^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n})\backslash T_{\mathbf{Ne}}U
, such that
f_{1}=a_{1}w_{0}+a_{2}w_{1}
,
f_{2}=b_{1}w_{0}+b_{2}w_{2}
for some constants
a_{1},b_{1},a_{2},b_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
and
w_{1},w_{2}\in T_{\mathbf{Ne}}U
.
Taking

c_{1}=c_{2}=1,\tilde{v}=0
in (
D.5
) and then
using Lemma
D.2
, (
D.9
), we get that
U(x)
given in (
1.13
)
attains
\inf\limits_{f\in\mathbf{Ne}}J[f]
.
By Lemma
D.1
and
B_{U}[f_{i},f_{i}]<0
,
i=1,2
, we have
a_{1}\neq 0,b_{1}\neq 0
.
So we can set
f_{3}:=f_{1}-b_{1}^{-1}a_{1}f_{2}\in T_{\mathbf{Ne}}U
. One using
B_{U}[f_{1},f_{2}]=0
, then
B_{U}[f_{3},f_{3}]=B_{U}[f_{1},f_{1}]+(b_{1}^{-1}a_{1})^{2}B_{U}[f_{2},f_{2}]<0
, which contradicts with
Lemma
D.1
.

Taking
\lambda_{0}=\lambda_{*}
and
Z_{0}=|f_{*}|
, we complete the proof.
∎
